/// The code of the app does not call it: it signs in through [appSession].
AuthService createAuthService() => _authService;

late AuthService _authService;

/// Creates the service of the provider of sign-in and starts the session of
/// the app with it; `bootstrap()` awaits it before the first frame, and
/// nothing else in the app calls it.
///
/// It may run again, as a test does to see what the next launch of the app
/// finds: it creates the service anew, with the user who is signed in, and
/// starts the session with it.
Future<void> initAuth() async {
  _authService = impl0.createFirebaseAuthService();
  await appSession.start(_authService);
}
=== lib/core/auth/auth_service.dart (role:auth) ===
/// A user as the provider of sign-in knows them.
final class AuthUser {
  /// Creates the user [uid].
  const AuthUser({
    required this.uid,
    required this.isAnonymous,
    this.email,
    this.issuers = const {},
  });

  /// The id of the user. It stays the same for as long as the user exists,
  /// also when an anonymous user gets an account.
  final String uid;

  /// Whether the user is anonymous: a user that the app signed in itself,
  /// who has no account and nothing to sign in with again.
  final bool isAnonymous;

  /// The email address of the user, or `null` for a user without one, such
  /// as an anonymous user.
  final String? email;

  /// The identity providers whose identities the user signs in with, each
  /// by the issuer of its tokens, such as `https://accounts.example.com`:
  /// the [IdTokenCredential.issuer] of a credential that the user signed in
  /// or linked with. It is empty for a user who signs in with a password
  /// alone, and for an anonymous user.
  final Set<String> issuers;
}

/// What a way to sign in got from an identity provider, such as the token
/// of an account of the user at another service, with which the provider
/// of sign-in signs the user in: [AuthService.signInWith] and
/// [AuthService.linkWith] take it.
sealed class SignInCredential {
  const SignInCredential();
}

/// An OpenID Connect ID token that an identity provider issued, with what
/// came with it.
final class IdTokenCredential extends SignInCredential {
  /// Creates the credential of [idToken], which [issuer] issued.
  const IdTokenCredential({
    required this.issuer,
    required this.idToken,
    this.rawNonce,
    this.authorizationCode,
    this.givenName,
    this.familyName,
  });

  /// The identity provider that issued the token: the `iss` of the token,
  /// an https URL such as `https://accounts.example.com`.
  final String issuer;

  /// The ID token, a JSON Web Token.
  final String idToken;

  /// The nonce whose hash the request for the token had, which the
  /// identity provider wrote into the token, or `null` if the request had
  /// none. The provider of sign-in passes it on where its backend checks
  /// the token against it.
  final String? rawNonce;

  /// The authorization code that came with the token, or `null`. With it, a
  /// provider of sign-in revokes the tokens that the identity provider gave
  /// the app when the account is deleted.
  final String? authorizationCode;

  /// The given name of the user, or `null`, for an identity provider that
  /// tells it apart from the token, and maybe only at the first sign-in.
  final String? givenName;

  /// The family name of the user, as [givenName].
  final String? familyName;

  /// Names the issuer, and no token, so that a token never reaches a log.
  @override
  String toString() => 'IdTokenCredential($issuer)';
}

/// Why a call of sign-in failed.
enum AuthFailureReason {
  /// The email address and the password match no account. A wrong password
  /// and an address without an account are this one reason, so that nobody
  /// learns from the app which addresses have an account.
  ///
  /// It is also the reason of a [SignInCredential] that the provider
  /// refuses, such as one that expired or that was issued for another app,
  /// and of one of another user than the one whose account is deleted.
  invalidCredentials,

  /// The email address has an account already.
  emailInUse,

  /// The identity of a [SignInCredential] belongs to another user already,
  /// so [AuthService.linkWith] gives it to nobody else. The session then
  /// signs in to that user.
  credentialInUse,

  /// The email address of the identity of a [SignInCredential] has an
  /// account that signs in in another way, such as with a password. The
  /// user signs in that way.
  accountExistsWithOtherMethod,

  /// The provider takes no password as weak as this one.
  weakPassword,

  /// The text is no email address.
  invalidEmail,

  /// The account is disabled.
  userDisabled,

  /// Too many attempts: the provider takes no more for a while.
  tooManyAttempts,

  /// The device did not reach the provider.
  network,

  /// The call needs a user who signed in a short while ago, as the deletion
  /// of an account does: the user signs in again and repeats it.
  ///
  /// It is also the reason of a call for a user whose session has ended on
  /// the server. The service signs that user out on the device before it
  /// fails.
  recentSignInRequired,

  /// Sign-in is not set up at the provider, such as a way to sign in that
  /// is not enabled there. This is a mistake in the setup of the app, which
  /// [AuthFailure.developerHint] explains.
  notConfigured,

  /// Anything else. [AuthFailure.developerHint] has the error.
  unknown,
}

/// The failure of a call of sign-in: the only thing that such a call throws.
final class AuthFailure implements Exception {
  /// Creates the failure for [reason].
  const AuthFailure(this.reason, {this.developerHint});

  /// Why the call failed. The app shows a text of its own for each reason.
  final AuthFailureReason reason;

  /// What went wrong in the words of the provider, for the developer of the
  /// app, or `null`. It is no text for the user: show it in debug mode
  /// only.
  final String? developerHint;

  @override
  String toString() => developerHint == null
      ? 'AuthFailure: ${reason.name}'
      : 'AuthFailure: ${reason.name} ($developerHint)';
}

/// The accounts of the users of the app and the user of this device, as the
/// provider of sign-in keeps them.
///
/// The provider implements it. The code of the app does not call it: it
/// signs in through `appSession` of `app_session.dart`, which calls this
/// service and does what is the same with every provider.
///
/// Every implementation keeps to this, in every mode of the app, whatever
/// the provider generates for the mode:
/// - The service has [currentUser] from the moment it is created: the user
///   who was signed in on this device when the app last ran, without a
///   request to the server. The function that creates the service may be
///   called again, and the service that it returns then has the user who is
///   signed in now.
/// - When the future of a call completes, [currentUser] is the result of
///   the call. [userChanges] tells of every change of [currentUser], also
///   of one that no call here made, and when it tells of a user,
///   [currentUser] is that user.
/// - A call fails only with an [AuthFailure], and every call ends: when the
///   server does not answer, its future completes with
///   [AuthFailureReason.network].
/// - A call that fails leaves the user who is signed in as that user was:
///   an anonymous user stays signed in when [linkPassword] or [linkWith]
///   fails, and so does whoever is signed in when [signIn], [signUp] or
///   [signInWith] fails. The one exception is a call for a user whose
///   session has ended on the server.
/// - [linkPassword], [linkWith] and [deleteAccount] are calls for the user
///   who is signed in. Made when nobody is signed in, or when the session of
///   that user has ended on the server, such a call fails with
///   [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired], and in the second case it
///   signs the user out on the device first.
/// - A [SignInCredential] whose identity provider the provider does not
///   take fails with [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured], with the issuer in
///   the [AuthFailure.developerHint].
abstract interface class AuthService {
  /// The user who is signed in on this device, or `null`.
  AuthUser? get currentUser;

  /// Tells of each change of [currentUser], once [currentUser] has changed:
  /// a broadcast stream. When it tells of a user, [currentUser] is that
  /// user. It may also tell of a user who has not changed.
  ///
  /// An error that it sends changes nothing: the session reports it as an
  /// error of the app and goes on listening.
  Stream<AuthUser?> get userChanges;

  /// Signs in to the account of [email] with [password].
  ///
  /// A wrong password and an address without an account both fail with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials].
  ///
  /// It is also called while a user is signed in, an anonymous one or the
  /// user of another account: the user of this account then replaces that
  /// user on the device. When it fails, that user stays signed in.
  Future<void> signIn({required String email, required String password});

  /// Creates an account for [email] with [password] and signs in to it.
  ///
  /// For an address that has an account already, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.emailInUse] and changes nothing: that account keeps
  /// its password, and whoever is signed in stays signed in.
  ///
  /// It is also called while the user of another account is signed in, and
  /// the user of the new account then replaces that user on the device.
  /// For an anonymous user, the session calls [linkPassword] instead.
  Future<void> signUp({required String email, required String password});

  /// Gives the anonymous user who is signed in an account for [email] with
  /// [password]. The user keeps the [AuthUser.uid] and is no longer
  /// anonymous.
  ///
  /// For an address that has an account already, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.emailInUse] and changes nothing: the anonymous user
  /// stays signed in, with the same id.
  Future<void> linkPassword({required String email, required String password});

  /// Signs in with [credential], which a way to sign in got from an
  /// identity provider: to the user of its identity, whom it creates when
  /// the identity is new.
  ///
  /// It is also called while a user is signed in, an anonymous one or the
  /// user of another account: the user of the identity then replaces that
  /// user on the device. When it fails, that user stays signed in.
  ///
  /// It takes a credential that [linkWith] refused just before, with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.credentialInUse] or
  /// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod]: the session passes
  /// the identical object. An identity provider may take a credential only
  /// once, so the service signs in with what its backend answered the
  /// refused link for that object, or with the credential itself when the
  /// backend gave nothing. For an identity whose email address has an
  /// account that signs in in another way, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod] or signs in to that
  /// account, as the backend of the provider decides.
  Future<void> signInWith(SignInCredential credential);

  /// Gives the user who is signed in the identity of [credential]: the user
  /// keeps the [AuthUser.uid], is no longer anonymous, and signs in with
  /// that identity from then on. The session calls it for an anonymous
  /// user.
  ///
  /// When the identity belongs to another user, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.credentialInUse], and when its email address has an
  /// account of another user, with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod]. Either way it changes
  /// nothing: the user stays signed in, with the same id.
  ///
  /// When it fails with either reason, it keeps what its backend answered
  /// for this credential object, such as a credential to sign in with,
  /// without holding the token longer than needed. The session passes the
  /// identical object to [signInWith], which uses that answer, or the
  /// credential itself when the backend gave nothing.
  Future<void> linkWith(SignInCredential credential);

  /// Creates an anonymous user, with an id of its own, and signs in as that
  /// user.
  Future<void> signInAnonymously();

  /// Sends the message with which the owner of the account of [email] sets
  /// a new password.
  ///
  /// It completes whether [email] has an account or not, so that nobody
  /// learns from the app which addresses have one.
  Future<void> sendPasswordReset(String email);

  /// Signs the user out on this device. It completes when nobody is signed
  /// in too.
  Future<void> signOut();

  /// Deletes the user who is signed in, an anonymous one too, and signs
  /// out.
  ///
  /// It succeeds for a user who has just signed in, in whichever way, and
  /// for an anonymous user who was just created. For a user who signed in
  /// longer ago, such as one whom the service found on the device when it
  /// was created, it may fail with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired]. It then succeeds once the
  /// user has signed in again, in whichever way the account allows.
  ///
  /// With [fresh], a credential that a way to sign in of the user got just
  /// before, the service first signs the user in again with it, so the
  /// deletion needs no recent sign-in, and revokes the tokens that the
  /// identity provider gave the app where its backend can, such as with
  /// [IdTokenCredential.authorizationCode]. A credential of another user
  /// fails with [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials], and a revocation
  /// that fails, such as one that the backend is not set up for, with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured]. Either way nothing is deleted.
  Future<void> deleteAccount({SignInCredential? fresh});
}
=== lib/core/auth/firebase_auth_service.dart (firebase_auth) ===
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:firebase_auth/firebase_auth.dart';
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart'
    show WidgetsBinding, basicLocaleListResolution;
import 'package:my_app/core/l10n/app_locale.dart';

import 'auth_service.dart';

/// Returns the sign-in service of the app on Firebase Authentication, with
/// the user who is signed in on this device.
///
/// Firebase is initialized first, as `bootstrap()` does. Firebase keeps the
/// user on the device and hands that user over when it is initialized, so
/// the service has the user at once, without a request to the server.
AuthService createFirebaseAuthService() =>
    FirebaseAuthService(FirebaseAuth.instance);

/// The codes with which Firebase answers a call for the user who is signed
/// in when the session of that user has ended on the server, as when the
/// account was deleted, or its password changed, on another device.
const _endedSession = {
  'user-token-expired',
  'invalid-user-token',
  'user-not-found',
  'no-current-user',
};

/// The codes of an answer of Firebase to a revocation that are no matter of
/// the setup of the Apple sign-in method of the project: a request that did
/// not reach Firebase, too many attempts, a disabled account, and a session
/// that has ended.
const _notOfSetup = {
  'network-request-failed',
  'too-many-requests',
  'user-disabled',
  ..._endedSession,
};

/// The issuer of the ID tokens of Google.
const _google = 'https://accounts.google.com';

/// The issuer of the ID tokens of Apple.
const _apple = 'https://appleid.apple.com';

/// The identity providers whose credentials the service takes, by the
/// issuer of their tokens: the id of each in Firebase Authentication, and
/// its name among the sign-in methods of the Firebase console.
const _providers = {
  _google: (id: 'google.com', name: 'Google'),
  _apple: (id: 'apple.com', name: 'Apple'),
};

/// The reasons of the codes with which Firebase refuses
/// [FirebaseAuthService.linkWith] because the identity of the credential,
/// or its email address, belongs to another user. For
/// [FirebaseAuthService.linkPassword], the same codes mean that the email
/// address has an account, `emailInUse`.
const _linkReasons = {
  'credential-already-in-use': AuthFailureReason.credentialInUse,
  'email-already-in-use': AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod,
  'account-exists-with-different-credential':
      AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod,
};

/// Sign-in with
/// [Firebase Authentication](https://firebase.google.com/docs/auth), with
/// an email address and a password, as an anonymous user, and with the
/// credentials that the ways to sign in of Google and Apple get.
///
/// The code of the app does not use it: it signs in through `appSession` of
/// `app_session.dart`.
///
/// The ways to sign in are enabled in the Firebase project, not here:
/// Email/Password, Anonymous for an app that signs in anonymous users, and
/// Google and Apple for an app with their ways to sign in.
/// `tool/enable_firebase_sign_in.dart` enables all but Apple. Until they
/// are, a call fails with [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured], and its
/// [AuthFailure.developerHint] tells how to enable them.
///
/// An empty address or password fails here, before Firebase is asked, since
/// Android and iOS answer it with different codes: an empty address with
/// [AuthFailureReason.invalidEmail], and an empty password with
/// [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials] in [signIn] and with
/// [AuthFailureReason.weakPassword] in [signUp] and [linkPassword].
///
/// A credential of Google goes to Firebase with its ID token, and one of
/// Apple with its ID token, its raw nonce and the name of the user:
/// `GoogleAuthProvider` takes no nonce, and Firebase does not check the
/// nonce of a token of Google. A credential of another identity
/// provider fails with [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured] before Firebase is
/// asked. Apple takes a credential once, so when Firebase refuses
/// [linkWith] because the identity or its email address belongs to another
/// user, the service keeps the credential that Firebase gives with its
/// answer, and [signInWith] of the identical credential object signs in
/// with that one.
///
/// [deleteAccount] with a fresh credential signs the user in again with it,
/// and, for a credential of Apple, revokes the tokens that Apple gave the
/// app with its authorization code before it deletes the account, as the
/// App Store asks of an app with Sign in with Apple. Firebase revokes them
/// on iOS and macOS, and has no call for it elsewhere. A revocation needs
/// the Services ID and the OAuth code flow configuration of the Apple
/// sign-in method in the Firebase console: without them it fails with
/// [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured], and the account stays.
final class FirebaseAuthService implements AuthService {
  /// Creates the service on [_auth].
  FirebaseAuthService(this._auth);

  final FirebaseAuth _auth;

  /// What Firebase gave with its answer to a link that it refused, for the
  /// credential object of the link: a credential of the same identity,
  /// which the plugin keeps on the platform and names by a number. It may
  /// carry the token that the platform gave, and is dropped once used. No
  /// hint names it.
  final Expando<AuthCredential> _givenForRefusedLink = Expando();

  /// Tells that the user may have changed: after each call here, and when
  /// Firebase tells of a change that no call here made, such as the end of
  /// the session of the user on the server. It follows Firebase only while
  /// it has a listener.
  late final StreamController<void> _changes = StreamController.broadcast(
    onListen: () => _fromFirebase = _auth.userChanges().listen(
      (_) => _changes.add(null),
      onError: _changes.addError,
    ),
    onCancel: () => _fromFirebase?.cancel(),
  );

  StreamSubscription<User?>? _fromFirebase;

  @override
  AuthUser? get currentUser {
    final user = _auth.currentUser;
    if (user == null) return null;
    final email = user.email;
    return AuthUser(
      uid: user.uid,
      isAnonymous: user.isAnonymous,
      // Android has an empty address, not none, for an anonymous user whom
      // it kept on the device.
      email: email == null || email.isEmpty ? null : email,
      issuers: {
        for (final MapEntry(key: issuer, value: (:id, name: _))
            in _providers.entries)
          if (user.providerData.any((info) => info.providerId == id)) issuer,
      },
    );
  }

  /// Each event has the user of the moment in which a listener gets it, so
  /// a listener never hears of a user who is not [currentUser] any more.
  @override
  Stream<AuthUser?> get userChanges => _changes.stream.map((_) => currentUser);

  @override
  Future<void> signIn({required String email, required String password}) =>
      _run(() {
        _checkGiven(email, password, AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials);
        return _auth.signInWithEmailAndPassword(
          email: email,
          password: password,
        );
      });

  @override
  Future<void> signUp({required String email, required String password}) =>
      _run(() {
        _checkGiven(email, password, AuthFailureReason.weakPassword);
        return _auth.createUserWithEmailAndPassword(
          email: email,
          password: password,
        );
      });

  @override
  Future<void> linkPassword({
    required String email,
    required String password,
  }) => _runForUser((user) {
    _checkGiven(email, password, AuthFailureReason.weakPassword);
    return user.linkWithCredential(
      EmailAuthProvider.credential(email: email, password: password),
    );
  });

  /// After a link of the identical [credential] that Firebase refused, it
  /// signs in with the credential that Firebase gave with that answer, once.
  @override
  Future<void> signInWith(SignInCredential credential) => _run(() {
    final given = _givenForRefusedLink[credential];
    _givenForRefusedLink[credential] = null;
    return _auth.signInWithCredential(given ?? _firebaseOf(credential));
  }, credential: credential);

  @override
  Future<void> linkWith(SignInCredential credential) => _runForUser(
    (user) async {
      final ofFirebase = _firebaseOf(credential);
      try {
        await user.linkWithCredential(ofFirebase);
      } on FirebaseAuthException catch (error) {
        // The sign-in that follows takes the credential that Firebase
        // gave: Apple takes the token of a credential once, and the
        // refused link has taken it.
        if (_linkReasons.containsKey(error.code)) {
          _givenForRefusedLink[credential] = error.credential;
        }
        rethrow;
      }
    },
    credential: credential,
    reasons: _linkReasons,
  );

  /// While an anonymous user is signed in, Firebase answers with that user
  /// and creates no other.
  @override
  Future<void> signInAnonymously() => _run(_auth.signInAnonymously);

  /// Firebase sends the message from the template of the project
  /// (Authentication > Templates in the Firebase console).
  @override
  Future<void> sendPasswordReset(String email) => _run(() async {
    _checkGiven(email);
    // The message is in the language that the app is in.
    await _auth.setLanguageCode(
      (appLocale.value ??
              basicLocaleListResolution(
                WidgetsBinding.instance.platformDispatcher.locales,
                appLocales,
              ))
          .languageCode,
    );
    try {
      await _auth.sendPasswordResetEmail(email: email);
    } on FirebaseAuthException catch (error) {
      // A project without the protection against the enumeration of
      // email addresses tells that an address has no account.
      if (error.code != 'user-not-found') rethrow;
    }
  });

  @override
  Future<void> signOut() => _run(_auth.signOut);

  @override
  Future<void> deleteAccount({SignInCredential? fresh}) =>
      _runForUser((user) async {
        if (fresh != null) await _signInAgain(user, fresh);
        await user.delete();
        // Firebase signs a deleted user out, but the plugin keeps that
        // user as the current one until the platform tells it of the
        // sign-out, which may come after this call is over.
        await _auth.signOut();
      }, credential: fresh);

  /// Signs [user] in again with [fresh], so that Firebase deletes the
  /// account without asking for a recent sign-in, and revokes the tokens of
  /// Apple with the authorization code of [fresh] where it does.
  Future<void> _signInAgain(User user, SignInCredential fresh) async {
    final ofFirebase = _firebaseOf(fresh);
    final code = _codeToRevoke(fresh);
    try {
      await user.reauthenticateWithCredential(ofFirebase);
    } on FirebaseAuthException catch (error) {
      if (error.code != 'user-not-found') rethrow;
      // Firebase answers so for a credential of an identity that has no
      // user, and for a credential of the user when the account was
      // deleted on the server, such as on another device. A reload of the
      // user fails for the second with a code of a session that has ended,
      // which signs the user out.
      await user.reload();
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials);
    }
    if (code != null) await _revoke(code);
  }

  /// The authorization code with which the service revokes the tokens that
  /// Apple gave the app before it deletes the account, for a [fresh]
  /// credential of Apple on iOS and macOS, or `null`: Firebase has no call
  /// for the revocation on the other platforms. Apple takes a code once, so
  /// it is the code of the fresh credential, never that of a sign-in
  /// before.
  String? _codeToRevoke(SignInCredential fresh) {
    if (fresh case IdTokenCredential(issuer: _apple, :final authorizationCode)
        when defaultTargetPlatform == TargetPlatform.iOS ||
            defaultTargetPlatform == TargetPlatform.macOS) {
      return authorizationCode ??
          (throw const AuthFailure(
            AuthFailureReason.notConfigured,
            developerHint:
                'The credential of Apple has no authorization '
                'code, with which Firebase revokes the tokens that Apple '
                'gave the app before the account is deleted, so the account '
                'was not deleted. The way to sign in with Apple gives one '
                'with each credential.',
          ));
    }
    return null;
  }

  /// Revokes the tokens that Apple gave the app with the authorization
  /// [code]. A failure keeps the account: it is
  /// [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured], but for an answer of Firebase that
  /// is no matter of the setup ([_notOfSetup]), which gets its reason as
  /// that of any other call.
  Future<void> _revoke(String code) async {
    // Firebase never completes the call while nobody is signed in.
    if (_auth.currentUser == null) {
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired);
    }
    try {
      await _auth.revokeTokenWithAuthorizationCode(code);
    } on Object catch (error) {
      if (error is FirebaseAuthException && _notOfSetup.contains(error.code)) {
        rethrow;
      }
      final project = _auth.app.options.projectId;
      throw AuthFailure(
        AuthFailureReason.notConfigured,
        developerHint:
            'Firebase did not revoke the tokens that Apple gave '
            'the app (${_answerOf(error)}), so the account was not deleted. '
            'The revocation needs the Services ID and the OAuth code flow '
            'configuration, with the Apple team ID, the Key ID and the '
            'private key, of the Apple sign-in method of the Firebase '
            'project $project, in the Firebase console under '
            'Authentication > Sign-in method: ${_consoleOf(project)}. When '
            'they are set, the authorization code of Apple was older than '
            'five minutes or used before, and the next deletion asks Apple '
            'for a new one.',
      );
    }
  }

  /// The credential of Firebase Authentication for [credential]. One of
  /// Google has the ID token alone: `GoogleAuthProvider` takes no nonce, and
  /// Firebase does not check the nonce of a token of Google.
  ///
  /// It fails with [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured] for a credential of an
  /// identity provider that the service does not take, and for a credential
  /// of Apple without the raw nonce, which Firebase checks the token of
  /// Apple against.
  AuthCredential _firebaseOf(SignInCredential credential) =>
      switch (credential) {
        IdTokenCredential(issuer: _google, :final idToken) =>
          GoogleAuthProvider.credential(idToken: idToken),
        IdTokenCredential(
          issuer: _apple,
          :final idToken,
          :final rawNonce?,
          :final givenName,
          :final familyName,
        ) =>
          AppleAuthProvider.credentialWithIDToken(
            idToken,
            rawNonce,
            AppleFullPersonName(givenName: givenName, familyName: familyName),
          ),
        IdTokenCredential(issuer: _apple) => throw const AuthFailure(
          AuthFailureReason.notConfigured,
          developerHint:
              'The credential of Apple has no raw nonce, which '
              'Firebase checks the token of Apple against. The way to sign '
              'in with Apple gives the nonce of its request with each '
              'credential.',
        ),
        IdTokenCredential(:final issuer) => throw AuthFailure(
          AuthFailureReason.notConfigured,
          developerHint:
              'The app signs in to Firebase Authentication with '
              'the credentials of Google ($_google) and Apple ($_apple), '
              'and takes no credential of $issuer.',
        ),
      };

  /// Runs [call] for the user who is signed in, as [_run] runs a call.
  ///
  /// It fails with [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired] when nobody is
  /// signed in, and when Firebase answers that the session of the user has
  /// ended: the user is then signed out on this device first.
  Future<void> _runForUser(
    Future<void> Function(User user) call, {
    SignInCredential? credential,
    Map<String, AuthFailureReason> reasons = const {},
  }) => _run(
    () async {
      final user = _auth.currentUser;
      if (user == null) {
        throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired);
      }
      try {
        await call(user);
      } on FirebaseAuthException catch (error) {
        if (!_endedSession.contains(error.code)) rethrow;
        await _auth.signOut();
        throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired);
      }
    },
    credential: credential,
    reasons: reasons,
  );

  /// Fails for an [email] that is empty with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.invalidEmail], and for a [password] that is empty
  /// with [withoutPassword], before Firebase is asked. Android refuses an
  /// empty text with a code that has no reason, and iOS answers an empty
  /// password as a wrong one, so the service decides for both.
  void _checkGiven(
    String email, [
    String? password,
    AuthFailureReason withoutPassword = AuthFailureReason.weakPassword,
  ]) {
    if (email.isEmpty) {
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.invalidEmail);
    }
    if (password != null && password.isEmpty) {
      throw AuthFailure(withoutPassword);
    }
  }

  /// Runs [call], which then fails only with an [AuthFailure], and tells
  /// of the user that it leaves, also when it failed.
  ///
  /// A code of Firebase gets its reason of [reasons], the reasons of the
  /// codes for this call, and otherwise that of [_failureOf]. [credential]
  /// is the credential that the call signs in with, whose way to sign in
  /// the hint of a failure names.
  Future<void> _run(
    Future<void> Function() call, {
    SignInCredential? credential,
    Map<String, AuthFailureReason> reasons = const {},
  }) async {
    try {
      await call();
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        _failureOf(error, credential, reasons),
        stackTrace,
      );
    } finally {
      _changes.add(null);
    }
  }

  /// The failure of a call that failed with [error]: the reason for the
  /// code of Firebase, from [reasons] first. A code that has no reason yet
  /// gets one here. The hint of a way to sign in that is not enabled names
  /// the way to sign in of [credential], or Email/Password and Anonymous.
  AuthFailure _failureOf(
    Object error,
    SignInCredential? credential,
    Map<String, AuthFailureReason> reasons,
  ) {
    if (error is AuthFailure) return error;
    if (error is! FirebaseAuthException) {
      return AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.unknown, developerHint: '$error');
    }
    final reason =
        reasons[error.code] ??
        switch (error.code) {
          // Firebase answers a wrong password and an address without an
          // account alike, unless the project is without the protection
          // against the enumeration of email addresses. It also answers so
          // for a credential that it refuses, such as one that expired, and
          // for one whose nonce it took before.
          'invalid-credential' ||
          'invalid-login-credentials' ||
          'wrong-password' ||
          'user-not-found' ||
          'missing-or-invalid-nonce' ||
          // A fresh credential of another user than the one whose account
          // is deleted.
          'user-mismatch' => AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials,
          'email-already-in-use' ||
          'credential-already-in-use' => AuthFailureReason.emailInUse,
          'account-exists-with-different-credential' =>
            AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod,
          'weak-password' || 'password-does-not-meet-requirements' =>
            AuthFailureReason.weakPassword,
          'invalid-email' || 'missing-email' => AuthFailureReason.invalidEmail,
          'user-disabled' => AuthFailureReason.userDisabled,
          'too-many-requests' => AuthFailureReason.tooManyAttempts,
          'network-request-failed' => AuthFailureReason.network,
          'requires-recent-login' => AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired,
          _ => null,
        };
    if (reason != null) return AuthFailure(reason);
    final answer = _answerOf(error);
    final project = _auth.app.options.projectId;
    final console =
        'the Firebase console under Authentication > Sign-in '
        'method: ${_consoleOf(project)}';
    final method = switch (credential) {
      IdTokenCredential(:final issuer) => _providers[issuer]?.name,
      null => null,
    };
    const script =
        'run `dart tool/enable_firebase_sign_in.dart` in the '
        'directory of the app';
    // The way to sign in, after "sign-in", and what enables it: the script
    // of the app enables Email/Password, Anonymous and Google, and the
    // Firebase console every way to sign in.
    final (withMethod, enable) = switch (method) {
      null => (
        '',
        '$script, or enable Email/Password, and Anonymous for an app that '
            'signs in anonymous users, in $console',
      ),
      'Google' => (' with Google', '$script, or enable Google in $console'),
      _ => (' with $method', 'enable $method in $console'),
    };
    // A way to sign in that is not enabled, which Firebase tells by its
    // code, and a project in which Authentication was never set up, which
    // it tells only in its message.
    const noConfiguration = 'CONFIGURATION_NOT_FOUND';
    final notEnabled = switch (error.code) {
      'operation-not-allowed' || 'admin-restricted-operation' => error.code,
      _ when answer.contains(noConfiguration) =>
        '${error.code}, $noConfiguration',
      _ => null,
    };
    if (notEnabled != null) {
      return AuthFailure(
        AuthFailureReason.notConfigured,
        developerHint:
            'Sign-in$withMethod is not enabled in the Firebase '
            'project $project (Firebase answered $notEnabled). To fix it, '
            '$enable',
      );
    }
    // On iOS and macOS, Firebase may answer for such a project with an
    // internal error whose message says no more, which nothing tells from
    // another internal error.
    if (error.code == 'internal-error' &&
        (defaultTargetPlatform == TargetPlatform.iOS ||
            defaultTargetPlatform == TargetPlatform.macOS)) {
      return AuthFailure(
        AuthFailureReason.notConfigured,
        developerHint:
            'Firebase reported an internal error ($answer), which '
            'is also what it answers on iOS and macOS when sign-in$withMethod '
            'is not enabled in the Firebase project $project. If that is the '
            'cause, $enable',
      );
    }
    return AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.unknown, developerHint: answer);
  }

  /// What Firebase answered with [error]: its code and its message on one
  /// line, since the message of iOS may be the description of an error over
  /// many lines, or the error itself when it is no error of Firebase.
  String _answerOf(Object error) {
    if (error is! FirebaseAuthException) return '$error';
    final message = (error.message ?? '')
        .replaceAll(RegExp(r'\s+'), ' ')
        .trim();
    return message.isEmpty ? error.code : '${error.code}: $message';
  }

  /// The page of the Firebase console with the sign-in methods of
  /// [project].
  String _consoleOf(String project) =>
      'https://console.firebase.google.com/project/$project/'
      'authentication/providers';
}
=== lib/core/auth/guest_data.dart (role:auth) ===
/// Gives the account [accountUid] what a guest had: the function that
/// [takeGuestData] returns.
typedef GiveGuestData = Future<void> Function(String accountUid);

/// Takes what the guest [guestUid] has in the app, and returns the function
/// that gives it to an account, or `null` when there is nothing to move.
///
/// A guest is an anonymous user. When a guest signs in to an account that
/// exists already, the user of the app changes from the guest to that
/// account, and what was kept under the id of the guest, such as a cart on
/// a server, would be left behind. So the session of the app calls this
/// function before the sign-in, while the guest is still signed in and may
/// read that data, and calls the function that it returns once the user of
/// the account is signed in:
///
/// ```dart
/// Future<GiveGuestData?> takeGuestData(String guestUid) async {
///   final cart = await carts.read(guestUid);
///   if (cart.isEmpty) return null;
///   return (accountUid) => carts.add(accountUid, cart);
/// }
/// ```
///
/// Only read here: the sign-in may still fail, and then the guest stays. An
/// error thrown here fails the sign-in before it starts. An error of the
/// function that this one returns is reported as an error of the app, and
/// the user stays signed in.
///
/// Call no method of `appSession` here, nor in the function that this one
/// returns. The sign-in is one of its calls, which run one after another,
/// so such a call would wait for the sign-in, and the sign-in for it.
///
/// It is not called when a guest signs up, since the guest then keeps the
/// id and everything under it, nor when a guest signs in with a way to sign
/// in whose identity no user has yet, which the guest then gets with the
/// id, nor in an app without anonymous users.
Future<GiveGuestData?> takeGuestData(String guestUid) async => null;
=== lib/core/auth/sign_in_methods.dart (role:auth) ===
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';
import 'package:my_app/core/apple_sign_in/apple_sign_in.dart' as method1;
import 'package:my_app/core/google_sign_in/google_sign_in.dart' as method0;
import 'package:my_app/core/l10n/l10n.dart';

import 'auth_service.dart' show SignInCredential;

/// A way to sign in with an identity provider, such as the account of the
/// user at another service: the label and the icon of its button, the
/// platforms that it works on, and the function that asks the identity
/// provider for a credential.
///
/// A screen of sign-in shows a button for each method of
/// [appSignInMethods] that [isAvailable]. On a tap, the state of the screen
/// calls [credential] and gives what it returns to `appSession.signInWith`
/// of `app_session.dart`.
final class SignInMethod {
  /// Creates a way to sign in with the identity provider [issuer].
  const SignInMethod({
    required this.label,
    required this.icon,
    required this.platforms,
    required this.credential,
    required this.issuer,
    this.revokesOnDeletion = false,
  });

  /// Returns the label of the button in the language of the app, for the
  /// context of the widget that shows it. That widget calls it each time it
  /// builds, so the text follows the language of the app.
  final String Function(BuildContext context) label;

  /// The icon of the button, a constant widget: an `Icon` takes the colour
  /// of the label, and an `Image` keeps its own colours.
  final Widget icon;

  /// The platforms that the way to sign in works on. On any other,
  /// [isAvailable] is false.
  final Set<TargetPlatform> platforms;

  /// Asks the identity provider for a credential, such as with a sheet of
  /// its own, and returns it, or `null` when the user closed the sheet,
  /// which is no failure. It fails only with an `AuthFailure`.
  ///
  /// The state of a screen calls it, and no code within a call of
  /// `appSession`: `appSession.deleteAccount()` calls it itself, for a
  /// method that [revokesOnDeletion].
  final Future<SignInCredential?> Function() credential;

  /// The identity provider: the issuer of its tokens, an https URL such as
  /// `https://accounts.example.com`. `AccountSession.issuers` of
  /// `app_session.dart` lists those of the user of an account.
  final String issuer;

  /// Whether the deletion of an account that signs in this way asks the
  /// identity provider for a credential again first, so that the provider
  /// of sign-in can revoke the tokens that the identity provider gave the
  /// app. `appSession.deleteAccount()` does it.
  final bool revokesOnDeletion;

  /// Whether the way to sign in works on the platform that the app runs on.
  bool get isAvailable => platforms.contains(defaultTargetPlatform);
}

/// The ways to sign in with an identity provider of the app, in the order
/// of their modules. An email address and a password are no such way:
/// `appSession.signIn` and `appSession.signUp` take them.
const List<SignInMethod> appSignInMethods = [
  SignInMethod(
    label: _googleSignInLabel,
    icon: method0.GoogleSignInIcon(),
    platforms: {TargetPlatform.android, TargetPlatform.iOS},
    credential: method0.googleCredential,
    issuer: 'https://accounts.google.com',
  ),
  SignInMethod(
    label: _appleSignInLabel,
    icon: method1.AppleSignInIcon(),
    platforms: {TargetPlatform.iOS},
    credential: method1.appleCredential,
    issuer: 'https://appleid.apple.com',
    revokesOnDeletion: true,
  ),
];

=== lib/core/crash_reporting/crash_reporter.dart (role:crash_reporting) ===
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:my_app/core/crash_reporting/crashlytics_crash_reporter.dart'
    as impl0;

/// Reports the errors of the app.
abstract interface class CrashReporter {
  /// Reports [error] with its [stackTrace]; a [fatal] error ended the app.
  Future<void> recordError(
    Object error,
    StackTrace? stackTrace, {
    bool fatal = false,
    String? reason,
  });

  /// Reports an error that Flutter caught, described by [details].
  Future<void> recordFlutterError(
    FlutterErrorDetails details, {
    bool fatal = false,
  });

  /// Adds [message] to the log sent with the next report.
  Future<void> log(String message);

  /// Sets the id of the signed-in user for the next reports, or clears it
  /// with `null`.
  Future<void> setUserId(String? userId);
}

/// Returns the crash reporter of the app, which forwards every call to the
/// crash reporters of all modules.
///
/// It calls each of them on its own, and its future completes once all of
/// them are done. A crash reporter that throws, or whose future fails,
/// keeps no other from the call, and its failure reaches neither the code
/// that called nor the handlers of [installCrashReporting], which would
/// report it to the same crash reporter again, without end. In debug mode
/// the failure is printed, so that a crash reporter that does not work,
/// such as one that is not set up, shows in the console. A crash reporter
/// whose factory throws, or that fails to start, is left out, and in debug
/// mode its error is printed.
CrashReporter createCrashReporter() => _crashReporter;

/// Reports the errors of the main isolate that nothing handles, each once.
///
/// `bootstrap()` calls it once the crash reporting services are ready. Two
/// handlers cover the main isolate:
/// - `FlutterError.onError` gets the errors that Flutter catches, such as in
///   a build or a layout, and still presents them as Flutter does by
///   default;
/// - `PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError` gets every other uncaught error,
///   such as in a `Future`, a `Timer` or the handler of a port, with its
///   type. In debug mode it leaves the error unhandled, so the engine prints
///   it.
///
/// Each report reaches every crash reporter, and the failure of a crash
/// reporter comes back to neither handler; see [createCrashReporter].
///
/// `compute()` and `Isolate.run()` throw the error of their isolate to the
/// code that awaits them, so it reaches the platform dispatcher unless that
/// code catches it. The uncaught errors of an isolate that the app spawns
/// itself go only to the error listener of that isolate, such as the
/// `onError` port of `Isolate.spawn`, so the app reports them there to its
/// crash reporter.
void installCrashReporting() {
  final reporter = createCrashReporter();
  final presentError = FlutterError.onError;
  FlutterError.onError = (details) {
    presentError?.call(details);
    unawaited(reporter.recordFlutterError(details, fatal: true));
  };
  PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError = (error, stackTrace) {
    unawaited(reporter.recordError(error, stackTrace, fatal: true));
    return !kDebugMode;
  };
}

final CrashReporter _crashReporter = _CrashReporters(_crashReporters);

final List<CrashReporter> _crashReporters = [
  ?_createAlone(
    'createCrashlyticsCrashReporter',
    impl0.createCrashlyticsCrashReporter,
  ),
];

/// Calls [create], the function [name], on its own: returns what it
/// creates, or `null` if it throws, so that the app works without it, and
/// in debug mode prints the error.
CrashReporter? _createAlone(String name, CrashReporter Function() create) {
  try {
    return create();
  } on Object catch (error) {
    if (kDebugMode) {
      debugPrint('$name() failed, so the app works without it: $error');
    }
    return null;
  }
}

final class _CrashReporters implements CrashReporter {
  const _CrashReporters(this._reporters);

  final List<CrashReporter> _reporters;

  @override
  Future<void> recordError(
    Object error,
    StackTrace? stackTrace, {
    bool fatal = false,
    String? reason,
  }) => _forAll(
    (reporter) =>
        reporter.recordError(error, stackTrace, fatal: fatal, reason: reason),
  );

  @override
  Future<void> recordFlutterError(
    FlutterErrorDetails details, {
    bool fatal = false,
  }) =>
      _forAll((reporter) => reporter.recordFlutterError(details, fatal: fatal));

  @override
  Future<void> log(String message) =>
      _forAll((reporter) => reporter.log(message));

  @override
  Future<void> setUserId(String? userId) =>
      _forAll((reporter) => reporter.setUserId(userId));

  /// Calls [call] with each crash reporter on its own, and completes once
  /// all of them are done, whatever each does; see [createCrashReporter].
  Future<void> _forAll(
    Future<void> Function(CrashReporter reporter) call,
  ) async {
    await Future.wait([
      for (final reporter in _reporters) _callAlone(reporter, call),
    ]);
  }

  /// Calls [call] with [reporter], and keeps what it throws, or the error of
  /// its future, from the caller: in debug mode it prints it.
  static Future<void> _callAlone(
    CrashReporter reporter,
    Future<void> Function(CrashReporter reporter) call,
  ) async {
    try {
      await call(reporter);
    } on Object catch (error) {
      if (kDebugMode) {
        debugPrint('The crash reporter ${reporter.runtimeType} failed: $error');
      }
    }
  }
}
=== lib/core/crash_reporting/crashlytics_crash_reporter.dart (firebase_crashlytics) ===
import 'package:firebase_crashlytics/firebase_crashlytics.dart';
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

import 'crash_reporter.dart';

/// Creates a crash reporter that reports to Firebase Crashlytics, which
/// needs Firebase to be initialized.
///
/// The app has one crash reporter, which `createCrashReporter()` returns and
/// which forwards every report to this one.
CrashReporter createCrashlyticsCrashReporter() =>
    CrashlyticsCrashReporter(FirebaseCrashlytics.instance);

/// Reports the errors of the app to Firebase Crashlytics.
///
/// It prints nothing. Of the errors that `installCrashReporting()` reports,
/// Flutter already presents those it catches, in every mode, and in debug
/// mode the engine prints the others, while Crashlytics would present each
/// error of Flutter again and, in debug mode, print each report. So an
/// error that the code of the app reports itself is not printed either.
final class CrashlyticsCrashReporter implements CrashReporter {
  /// Creates the reporter on the given [FirebaseCrashlytics].
  CrashlyticsCrashReporter(this._crashlytics);

  final FirebaseCrashlytics _crashlytics;

  @override
  Future<void> recordError(
    Object error,
    StackTrace? stackTrace, {
    bool fatal = false,
    String? reason,
  }) => _crashlytics.recordError(
    error,
    stackTrace,
    reason: reason,
    printDetails: false,
    fatal: fatal,
  );

  /// Reports what [FirebaseCrashlytics.recordFlutterError] reports, without
  /// presenting the error as it also does.
  @override
  Future<void> recordFlutterError(
    FlutterErrorDetails details, {
    bool fatal = false,
  }) => _crashlytics.recordError(
    details.exceptionAsString(),
    details.stack,
    reason: details.context
        ?.toStringDeep(minLevel: DiagnosticLevel.info)
        .trim(),
    information: details.informationCollector?.call() ?? const [],
    printDetails: false,
    fatal: fatal,
  );

  @override
  Future<void> log(String message) => _crashlytics.log(message);

  /// Sets the id of the user, or clears it with `null`, which Crashlytics
  /// does with an empty id.
  @override
  Future<void> setUserId(String? userId) =>
      _crashlytics.setUserIdentifier(userId ?? '');
}

/// value, or `null` while it is empty or missing.
///
/// A command of the `flutter` tool that builds, runs or tests the app reads
/// `.env` when it starts, given `--dart-define-from-file=.env`, and compiles

  /// test_, for debug builds only.

/// A message that one part of the app sends to the others, such as
/// `final class ItemAdded extends AppEvent`.
abstract class AppEvent {
  /// Allows the events to have constant constructors.
  const AppEvent();
}

/// Delivers [AppEvent]s between parts of the app that do not know each
/// other.
///
/// Whether a listener of a type that an event extends or implements, such
/// as a listener of `on<AppEvent>()`, gets the event is up to the provider
/// of the service.
abstract interface class CommunicationService {
  /// Sends [event] to everyone listening to its type when it is fired.
  void fire(AppEvent event);

  /// The events of type [T] fired after the stream is listened to. An event
  /// fired before, even after this call returned the stream, is not in it.
  Stream<T> on<T extends AppEvent>();
}

CommunicationService createCommunicationService() => _communicationService;

    .createEventBusCommunicationService();

=== lib/core/google_sign_in/google_sign_in.dart (google_sign_in) ===
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';
import 'package:google_sign_in/google_sign_in.dart';

import '../auth/app_session.dart';

/// The issuer of the ID tokens of Google.
const String _issuer = 'https://accounts.google.com';

/// The logo of Google, the "G", an image that `pubspec.yaml` declares among
/// the assets of the app. Flutter takes the file of the same name in
/// `2.0x/` and `3.0x/` next to it for a screen with more pixels.
const String _logo = 'lib/core/google_sign_in/assets/google_logo.png';

/// The start of google_sign_in, which [googleCredential] waits for: the
/// package is started once, before its first sign-in.
Future<void>? _start;

/// Whether [_start] completed. A call after that does not wait for it
/// again: a future that completed lets each call that awaits it go on in a
/// microtask of the zone that started it, which may run none any more, as
/// the fake time of a widget test that is over before a later test signs in
/// in real time.
bool _started = false;

/// Asks Google for a credential with the sheet of Google sign-in, and
/// returns it, or `null` when the user closed the sheet.
///
/// The first call starts google_sign_in with `GoogleSignIn.instance
/// .initialize()`, without the ids of the clients of Google, so the package
/// finds them in the files of the app where the provider of sign-in puts
/// them. The credential is the ID token of the Google account that the user
/// chose.
///
/// A client of Google that is missing in the app, or a Google project that
/// does not know the app, fails with `notConfigured`, with a hint at the
/// section of the README of the app on it, "Google sign-in". Any other error
/// fails with `unknown`. On iOS, the Google Sign-In SDK stops the app
/// instead when the app has no iOS client of Google or not its URL scheme,
/// since the package does not catch what the SDK throws then.
Future<SignInCredential?> googleCredential() async {
  final GoogleSignInAccount account;
  try {
    if (!_started) {
      await (_start ??= GoogleSignIn.instance.initialize());
      _started = true;
    }
    account = await GoogleSignIn.instance.authenticate();
  } on GoogleSignInException catch (error) {
    switch (error.code) {
      case GoogleSignInExceptionCode.canceled:
        return null;
      case GoogleSignInExceptionCode.clientConfigurationError:
      case GoogleSignInExceptionCode.providerConfigurationError:
        throw _notConfigured(error.description ?? error.code.name);
      // Credential Manager on Android answers so when the Google project
      // has no Android client with the name of the package and the SHA-1
      // of the key that signed the app.
      case GoogleSignInExceptionCode.unknownError
          when RegExp(r'\b28444\b').hasMatch(error.description ?? ''):
        throw _notConfigured(error.description!);
      default:
        throw AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.unknown, developerHint: '$error');
    }
  } on Object catch (error) {
    throw AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.unknown, developerHint: '$error');
  }
  final idToken = account.authentication.idToken;
  if (idToken == null) {
    throw const AuthFailure(
      AuthFailureReason.unknown,
      developerHint: 'Google gave no ID token.',
    );
  }
  return IdTokenCredential(issuer: _issuer, idToken: idToken);
}

/// The failure of a sign-in with Google that the app or the Google project
/// is not set up for, with [message], what Google answered.
AuthFailure _notConfigured(String message) => AuthFailure(
  AuthFailureReason.notConfigured,
  developerHint:
      'Google sign-in is not set up for the app ($message). See the '
      'section "Google sign-in" of the README of the app.',
);

/// The icon of the button of Google sign-in: the "G" of Google in its own
/// colours, at the size of the icons of the button, as the branding
/// guidelines of Google ask of a custom button.
class GoogleSignInIcon extends StatelessWidget {
  /// Creates the icon.
  const GoogleSignInIcon({super.key});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final size = IconTheme.of(context).size;
    // The label of the button names Google.
    return Image.asset(
      _logo,
      width: size,
      height: size,
      excludeFromSemantics: true,
    );
  }
}
=== lib/core/l10n/app_locale.dart (role:localization) ===
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import '../preferences/app_preferences.dart';

/// The languages of the app. The app shows its texts in the language that
/// the device prefers among them, and in the first of the list when the
/// device asks for none of them.
///
/// A language has one locale here. The app tells its languages apart by
/// their codes alone, so of two locales of one language, such as
/// `Locale('pt', 'BR')` and `Locale('pt', 'PT')`, `appLocale.choose()`
/// takes the first, whichever of them it is given.
const appLocales = <Locale>[Locale('en'), Locale('uk')];

/// The key of the preferences of the app under which the app saves the
/// language that the user chose, as the code of the language, such as `uk`.
/// Nothing is saved under it while the app follows the languages of the
/// device.
const _key = 'localization.locale';

/// The language that the user chose for the app, which the app remembers
/// between its launches.
///
/// The root of the app reads it through [AppLocaleScope], and rebuilds in
/// the new language when the choice changes.
final appLocale = AppLocaleController._();

/// Keeps the language that the user chose for the app, and saves each
/// choice in the preferences of the app.
final class AppLocaleController extends ChangeNotifier {
  AppLocaleController._();

  Locale? _locale;

  /// The preferences of the app, once it opened them.
  AppPreferences? _preferences;

  /// The language that the user chose, one of [appLocales], or `null` while
  /// the app follows the languages of the device.
  Locale? get value => _locale;

  /// Chooses the language of [locale] as the language of the app, or with
  /// `null` lets the app follow the languages of the device again.
  ///
  /// The choice is the one of [appLocales] with the language of [locale],
  /// so `Locale('uk', 'UA')` chooses `Locale('uk')`. A locale of a language
  /// that the app is not in is refused: the future completes with an
  /// [ArgumentError], and nothing changes.
  ///
  /// The language of the app changes at once, and the choice is then saved
  /// in the preferences once the app opened them: before that, it changes
  /// only memory. When saving fails, the future completes with the error of
  /// the preferences. The app stays in the new language while it runs, and
  /// its next launch starts with what was saved before; choosing the
  /// language again saves it again.
  Future<void> choose(Locale? locale) async {
    final chosen = locale == null ? null : _ofApp(locale.languageCode);
    if (locale != null && chosen == null) {
      throw ArgumentError.value(
        '$locale',
        'locale',
        'The app is not in the language of this locale',
      );
    }
    _set(chosen);
    final preferences = _preferences;
    if (preferences == null) return;
    if (chosen == null) {
      await preferences.remove(_key);
    } else {
      await preferences.setString(_key, chosen.languageCode);
    }
  }

  void _set(Locale? locale) {
    if (locale == _locale) return;
    _locale = locale;
    notifyListeners();
  }
}

/// The one of [appLocales] with the language of the code [code], or `null`.
Locale? _ofApp(String? code) {
  for (final locale in appLocales) {
    if (locale.languageCode == code) return locale;
  }
  return null;
}

/// Restores the language that the user chose from [preferences], which the
/// app opened, and keeps them for the choices to come: a restorer of the
/// preferences, which the app calls before its first frame.
///
/// When nothing is saved, or what is saved is not the code of one of
/// [appLocales], the choice stays as it is.
void restoreAppLocale(AppPreferences preferences) {
  appLocale._preferences = preferences;
  final saved = _ofApp(preferences.getString(_key));
  if (saved != null) appLocale._set(saved);
}

/// Gives the root of the app the language that the user chose, and rebuilds
/// the root when the choice changes.
class AppLocaleScope extends InheritedNotifier<AppLocaleController> {
  /// Creates the scope of [notifier] around [child].
  const AppLocaleScope({
    required AppLocaleController super.notifier,
    required super.child,
    super.key,
  });

  /// The language that the user chose, or `null` to follow the device; the
  /// widget of [context] rebuilds when the choice changes.
  static Locale? of(BuildContext context) => context
      .dependOnInheritedWidgetOfExactType<AppLocaleScope>()!
      .notifier!
      .value;
}

=== lib/core/l10n/language_setting.dart (role:localization) ===
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:my_app/core/l10n/l10n.dart';

import 'app_locale.dart';

/// The names of the languages of the app, each in its own language, by the
/// code of the language. The setting shows a language of the app without a
/// name here by its code: add the name of such a language.
const _names = <String, String>{'en': 'English', 'uk': 'Українська'};

/// The setting of the language of the app, an entry of the settings screen:
/// it shows the language that the user chose, and opens a sheet in which
/// the user chooses one of the languages of the app, or follows the
/// languages of the device.
///
/// The entry does not wait until a choice is saved. If the preferences fail
/// to save it, the app is in the chosen language while it runs, its next
/// launch starts with what was saved before, and the error is one that
/// nothing here catches: it reaches the handlers of the uncaught errors of
/// the app.
class LanguageSetting extends StatelessWidget {
  /// Creates the setting.
  const LanguageSetting({super.key});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final theme = Theme.of(context);
    final muted = theme.colorScheme.onSurfaceVariant;
    final chosen = AppLocaleScope.of(context);
    final choice = Text(
      chosen == null ? context.l10n.localizationSystem : _nameOf(chosen),
      style: theme.textTheme.bodyMedium?.copyWith(color: muted),
    );
    final arrow = Icon(Icons.chevron_right, color: muted);
    // At a large text size, the choice goes below the title, where it has
    // the width of the row.
    final below = MediaQuery.textScalerOf(context).scale(10) > 13;
    return ListTile(
      leading: const Icon(Icons.language),
      title: Text(context.l10n.localizationLanguage),
      subtitle: below ? choice : null,
      trailing: below
          ? arrow
          : Row(
              mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
              children: [choice, const SizedBox(width: 4), arrow],
            ),
      onTap: () => showModalBottomSheet<void>(
        context: context,
        // Over the main navigation of the app too.
        useRootNavigator: true,
        showDragHandle: true,
        builder: (context) => const _LanguageSheet(),
      ),
    );
  }
}

/// The name of the language of [locale] in that language, or its code.
String _nameOf(Locale locale) =>
    _names[locale.languageCode] ?? locale.languageCode;

/// The sheet of the setting: the choice between the languages of the app
/// and those of the device. It scrolls where its options do not fit.
class _LanguageSheet extends StatelessWidget {
  const _LanguageSheet();

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final theme = Theme.of(context);
    final chosen = AppLocaleScope.of(context);
    return SafeArea(
      child: ListView(
        // A drag of options that fit closes the sheet, as a drag of its
        // handle does.
        primary: false,
        shrinkWrap: true,
        children: [
          Padding(
            padding: const EdgeInsets.fromLTRB(24, 0, 24, 12),
            child: Semantics(
              header: true,
              child: Text(
                context.l10n.localizationLanguage,
                style: theme.textTheme.headlineSmall,
              ),
            ),
          ),
          for (final Locale? locale in [null, ...appLocales])
            ListTile(
              contentPadding: const EdgeInsets.symmetric(
                horizontal: 24,
                vertical: 4,
              ),
              title: Text(
                locale == null
                    ? context.l10n.localizationSystem
                    : _nameOf(locale),
                // Larger than the text of a row of the screen.
                style: theme.textTheme.bodyLarge?.copyWith(fontSize: 19),
              ),
              selected: locale == chosen,
              trailing: locale == chosen
                  ? Icon(
                      Icons.check_rounded,
                      color: theme.colorScheme.secondary,
                    )
                  : null,
              onTap: () async {
                Navigator.of(context).pop();
                await appLocale.choose(locale);
              },
            ),
          const SizedBox(height: 8),
        ],
      ),
    );
  }
}

import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';

import 'destination.dart';

/// The least height of the bar above the inset of the device. The bar grows
/// when a label needs more.
const double _barHeight = 64;

/// The most that the text size of the device scales the label of a tab. A
/// long press on a tab shows its label in a tooltip, at the full text size.
const double _maxLabelScale = 1.3;

/// The main navigation of the app: the screen of the selected destination,
/// and below it a bar with a tab for each destination.
///
/// With one destination there is nothing to switch to, so the bar is
/// hidden and the screen fills the app.
class AppShell extends StatelessWidget {
  /// Creates the main navigation with [destinations], the one at
  /// [currentIndex] selected with [body] as its screen; selecting a tab
  /// calls [onSelect] with its index.
  const AppShell({
    required this.destinations,
    required this.currentIndex,
    required this.onSelect,
    required this.body,
    super.key,
  });

  /// The tabs of the bar, in order.
  final List<Destination> destinations;

  /// The index of the selected destination.
  final int currentIndex;

  /// Selects the destination at the given index.
  final ValueChanged<int> onSelect;

  /// The screen of the selected destination.
  final Widget body;

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) => Scaffold(
    body: _BranchTransition(index: currentIndex, child: body),
    bottomNavigationBar: destinations.length < 2
        ? null
        : _TabBar(
            destinations: destinations,
            currentIndex: currentIndex,
            onTap: _tapped,
          ),
  );

  /// Selects the destination of the tab at [index], which the user tapped.
  /// The device gives a light tick when that tab is not the selected one.
  void _tapped(int index) {
    if (index != currentIndex) HapticFeedback.selectionClick();
    onSelect(index);
  }
}

/// The bar at the bottom: a tab for each destination, below a hairline.
class _TabBar extends StatelessWidget {
  const _TabBar({
    required this.destinations,
    required this.currentIndex,
    required this.onTap,
  });

  final List<Destination> destinations;
  final int currentIndex;
  final ValueChanged<int> onTap;

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final colors = Theme.of(context).colorScheme;
    // A Material of its own, so that the ink of a tab shows on the bar and
    // stays inside it.
    return Material(
      color: NavigationBarTheme.of(context).backgroundColor ?? colors.surface,
      shape: Border(top: BorderSide(color: colors.outlineVariant)),
      child: SafeArea(
        top: false,
        child: Row(
          children: [
            for (final (index, destination) in destinations.indexed)
              Expanded(
                child: _Tab(
                  destination: destination,
                  selected: index == currentIndex,
                  onTap: () => onTap(index),
                ),
              ),
          ],
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}

/// The tab of [destination]: its icon over its label. When it becomes the
/// selected one, both take the colour of the selected destination, and
/// neither changes its size.
class _Tab extends StatelessWidget {
  const _Tab({
    required this.destination,
    required this.selected,
    required this.onTap,
  });

  final Destination destination;
  final bool selected;
  final VoidCallback onTap;

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final theme = Theme.of(context);
    final colors = theme.colorScheme;
    // Read here each time the tab builds, so the label follows the language
    // of the app.
    final label = destination.label(context);
    // The colours that the theme of the app gives the icons of a navigation
    // bar, or else its primary colour for the selected tab.
    final ofTheme = NavigationBarTheme.of(context).iconTheme;
    final color = selected
        ? ofTheme?.resolve(const {WidgetState.selected})?.color ??
              colors.primary
        : ofTheme?.resolve(const {})?.color ?? colors.onSurfaceVariant;
    // For a user who asks the device for less motion, the tab changes at
    // once.
    final duration = MediaQuery.disableAnimationsOf(context)
        ? Duration.zero
        : Durations.medium2;
    // One node for a screen reader: a button with the label of the
    // destination, selected or not.
    return Semantics(
      container: true,
      button: true,
      selected: selected,
      label: label,
      child: Tooltip(
        message: label,
        excludeFromSemantics: true,
        preferBelow: false,
        verticalOffset: 40,
        child: InkResponse(
          onTap: onTap,
          radius: 44,
          child: ConstrainedBox(
            constraints: const BoxConstraints(minHeight: _barHeight),
            child: Padding(
              padding: const EdgeInsets.symmetric(horizontal: 4),
              child: ExcludeSemantics(
                child: TweenAnimationBuilder<Color?>(
                  tween: ColorTween(end: color),
                  duration: duration,
                  builder: (context, color, _) => Column(
                    mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
                    mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.center,
                    children: [
                      Icon(destination.icon, size: 24, color: color),
                      const SizedBox(height: 4),
                      MediaQuery.withClampedTextScaling(
                        maxScaleFactor: _maxLabelScale,
                        child: Text(
                          label,
                          maxLines: 1,
                          overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis,
                          style: theme.textTheme.labelMedium?.copyWith(
                            color: color,
                          ),
                        ),
                      ),
                    ],
                  ),
                ),
              ),
            ),
          ),
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}

/// Shows [child], the screen of the destination at [index], and lets it
/// fade in while it rises a little, once, when the destination changes.
class _BranchTransition extends StatefulWidget {
  const _BranchTransition({required this.index, required this.child});

  final int index;
  final Widget child;

  @override
  State<_BranchTransition> createState() => _BranchTransitionState();
}

class _BranchTransitionState extends State<_BranchTransition>
    with SingleTickerProviderStateMixin {
  /// At its end while no destination changes, where the screen is shown as
  /// it is.
  late final AnimationController _controller = AnimationController(
    vsync: this,
    duration: Durations.medium2,
    value: 1,
  );

  @override
  void didUpdateWidget(_BranchTransition oldWidget) {
    super.didUpdateWidget(oldWidget);
    if (oldWidget.index == widget.index) return;
    // For a user who asks the device for less motion, the screen is shown
    // at once.
    if (MediaQuery.disableAnimationsOf(context)) {
      _controller.value = 1;
    } else {
      _controller.forward(from: 0);
    }
  }

  @override
  void dispose() {
    _controller.dispose();
    super.dispose();
  }

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) => AnimatedBuilder(
    animation: _controller,
    builder: (context, child) {
      final shown = Easing.emphasizedDecelerate.transform(_controller.value);
      return Opacity(
        opacity: shown,
        child: Transform.translate(
          offset: Offset(0, (1 - shown) * 10),
          child: child,
        ),
      );
    },
    child: widget.child,
  );
}
