/// for the network, so that the access comes well within the time that the
/// start of the purchases waits for it.
const _offeringsWait = Duration(seconds: 1);

/// The purchases of the app on [RevenueCat](https://www.revenuecat.com),
/// with purchases_flutter.
///
/// The code of the app does not use it: it buys and restores through
/// `appPurchases` of `app_purchases.dart`.
///
/// The service configures RevenueCat when it is created, with the key of
/// [RevenueCatKey], and every call waits for that: RevenueCat stops an iOS
/// app that calls it before it is configured. Without a key it never calls
/// RevenueCat, and every call fails with
/// [PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured] and the hint of the key.
///
/// The premium access of the app is the entitlement `premium` in
/// RevenueCat, which every product of the current offering unlocks. The id
/// of a [Plan] is the identifier of its package in that offering.
final class RevenueCatPurchasesService implements PurchasesService {
  /// Creates the service, which configures RevenueCat with [key], if it has
  /// a value.
  RevenueCatPurchasesService(this.key) {
    final value = key.value;
    _configured = value == null ? Future.value(false) : _configure(value);
  }

  /// The key that the service configures RevenueCat with, or why it has
  /// none.
  final RevenueCatKey key;

  /// Completes with whether RevenueCat is configured, once it is or failed.
  late final Future<bool> _configured;

  /// Completes with whether the service configured RevenueCat, once it did
  /// or failed: `false` without a key, or when RevenueCat refused the key.
  Future<bool> get configured => _configured;

  /// Why configuring RevenueCat failed, or `null`.
  String? _configureHint;

  /// The packages of the current offering that [plans] gave, by their
  /// identifiers, which a purchase needs.
  final Map<String, Package> _packages = {};

  /// Configures RevenueCat with [value], and completes with whether it did.
  /// It never fails, since nothing may wait for it.
  Future<bool> _configure(String value) async {
    try {
      await Purchases.configure(PurchasesConfiguration(value));
    } on PlatformException catch (error) {
      _configureHint =
          'RevenueCat refused to start with ${key.name}: ${error.message}';
      return false;
    } on MissingPluginException catch (error) {
      _configureHint =
          'RevenueCat did not start with ${key.name}: the app '
          'has no platform side of purchases_flutter, as in a test without '
          'mocks of the method channel purchases_flutter (${error.message}).';
      return false;
    } on Object catch (error) {
      _configureHint = 'RevenueCat did not start with ${key.name}: $error';
      return false;
    }
    if (!_listening) {
      _listening = true;
      Purchases.addCustomerInfoUpdateListener(_customerInfos.add);
    }
    return true;
  }

  /// Runs [call] once RevenueCat is configured, and turns what it fails
  /// with into a [PurchasesFailure]: an error of RevenueCat into the reason
  /// of its code, and any other error into the reason
  /// [PurchasesFailureReason.unknown].
  Future<T> _call<T>(Future<T> Function() call) async {
    if (!await _configured) {
      throw PurchasesFailure(
        PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured,
        developerHint: key.hint ?? _configureHint,
      );
    }
    try {
      return await call();
    } on PurchasesFailure {
      rethrow;
    } on PlatformException catch (error, stackTrace) {
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(_failureOf(error, key.name), stackTrace);
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        PurchasesFailure(
          PurchasesFailureReason.unknown,
          developerHint: '$error',
        ),
        stackTrace,
      );
    }
  }

  @override
  Future<void> identify(String? userId) => _call(() async {
    if (userId != null) {
      await Purchases.logIn(userId);
    } else if (!await Purchases.isAnonymous) {
      try {
        await Purchases.logOut();
      } on PlatformException catch (error) {
        // RevenueCat has an anonymous user already, as after another
        // call that logged out meanwhile: that is the user of this
        // device.
        if (_codeOf(error) != PurchasesErrorCode.logOutWithAnonymousUserError) {
          rethrow;
        }
      }
    }
  });

  @override
  Future<AppAccess> access() =>
      _call(() async => _accessAfter(await Purchases.getCustomerInfo()));

  @override
  Stream<AppAccess> get accessChanges => key.value == null
      ? const Stream<AppAccess>.empty()
      : _customerInfos.stream.map(_accessOf);

  @override
  Future<List<Plan>> plans() => _call(() async {
    final offerings = await Purchases.getOfferings();
    _read(offerings);
    final current = offerings.current;
    final packages = current?.availablePackages ?? const <Package>[];
    if (current == null || packages.isEmpty) {
      throw PurchasesFailure(
        PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured,
        developerHint: current == null
            ? 'RevenueCat has no current offering: make the offering with '
                  'the packages of the app current in RevenueCat.'
            : 'The current offering ${current.identifier} of RevenueCat '
                  'has no packages: add the packages of the app to it.',
      );
    }
    _packages
      ..clear()
      ..addEntries([
        for (final package in packages) MapEntry(package.identifier, package),
      ]);
    final withIntro = await _withIntro(packages);
    return [for (final package in packages) _planOf(package, withIntro)];
  });

  @override
  Future<AppAccess?> purchase(Plan plan) => _call(() async {
    final package = _packages[plan.id] ?? await _packageAgain(plan.id);
    final CustomerInfo info;
    try {
      info = (await Purchases.purchase(
        PurchaseParams.package(package),
      )).customerInfo;
    } on PlatformException catch (error) {
      if (_cancelled(error)) return null;
      rethrow;
    }
    final access = await _accessAfter(info);
    if (access is! PremiumAccess) {
      throw PurchasesFailure(
        PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured,
        developerHint:
            'The user bought '
            '${package.storeProduct.identifier}, but RevenueCat gives no '
            'active entitlement $_entitlement for it: attach the product '
            'to the entitlement $_entitlement in RevenueCat, and a '
            'restore gives the access.',
      );
    }
    return access;
  });

  @override
  Future<AppAccess> restore() =>
      _call(() async => _accessAfter(await Purchases.restorePurchases()));

  /// The package [id] of the current offering, which RevenueCat gives again.
  /// Fails with [PurchasesFailureReason.unavailable] when the offering has
  /// none of that id.
  Future<Package> _packageAgain(String id) async {
    final offerings = await Purchases.getOfferings();
    _read(offerings);
    final package = offerings.current?.getPackage(id);
    if (package == null) {
      throw PurchasesFailure(
        PurchasesFailureReason.unavailable,
        developerHint:
            'The current offering of RevenueCat has no package '
            '$id.',
      );
    }
    return _packages[id] = package;
  }

  /// The access of [info], once the app read the offerings that tell the
  /// period of a plan that renews, if it has not yet. It waits for them at
  /// most [_offeringsWait]: the access then has no period, and the periods
  /// come with offerings that RevenueCat gives later.
  Future<AppAccess> _accessAfter(CustomerInfo info) async {
    final premium = info.entitlements.active[_entitlement];
    if (premium?.expirationDate != null && !_periodsRead) {
      final read = Purchases.getOfferings().then(_read);
      try {
        await read.timeout(_offeringsWait);
      } on Object {
        // The access then has no period, as when the offerings do not have
        // its product. A read that ends later keeps the periods, or fails
        // with nobody to tell.
        unawaited(read.then((_) {}, onError: (Object _) {}));
      }
    }
    return _accessOf(info);
  }
}

/// Keeps the periods of the products of [offerings].
void _read(Offerings offerings) {
  for (final offering in offerings.all.values) {
    for (final package in offering.availablePackages) {
      final product = package.storeProduct;
      final period = _periodIn(product.subscriptionPeriod);
      if (period != null) _periods[product.identifier] = period;
    }
  }
  _periodsRead = true;
}

/// The access of the customer of [info]: premium while the entitlement
/// [_entitlement] is active.
AppAccess _accessOf(CustomerInfo info) {
  final premium = info.entitlements.active[_entitlement];
  if (premium == null || !premium.isActive) return const NoAccess();
  final expiration = premium.expirationDate;
  final manageUrl = info.managementURL;
  return PremiumAccess(
    expiresAt: expiration == null ? null : DateTime.tryParse(expiration),
    renews: premium.willRenew,
    inTrial: premium.periodType == PeriodType.trial,
    period: expiration == null ? null : _periodOf(premium),
    manageUrl: manageUrl == null ? null : Uri.tryParse(manageUrl),
  );
}

/// How often the product of [premium] is billed, if the offerings have
/// it. On Android the product of a plan in the store is the subscription
/// and its base plan.
BillingPeriod? _periodOf(EntitlementInfo premium) {
  final product = premium.productIdentifier;
  final basePlan = premium.productPlanIdentifier;
  return (basePlan == null ? null : _periods['$product:$basePlan']) ??
      _periods[product];
}

/// The ids of the products of [packages] whose intro offer the user may
/// take. Android gives a product only the offers that the user may take,
/// and on iOS the app asks: it shows an offer only when iOS says the user
/// may take it, so that it promises no trial that the store does not give.
Future<Set<String>> _withIntro(List<Package> packages) async {
  final ids = [
    for (final package in packages)
      if (package.storeProduct.introductoryPrice != null)
        package.storeProduct.identifier,
  ];
  if (ids.isEmpty || defaultTargetPlatform != TargetPlatform.iOS) {
    return ids.toSet();
  }
  try {
    final eligibility =
        await Purchases.checkTrialOrIntroductoryPriceEligibility(ids);
    return {
      for (final MapEntry(key: id, value: of) in eligibility.entries)
        if (of.status == IntroEligibilityStatus.introEligibilityStatusEligible)
          id,
    };
  } on PlatformException {
    return const {};
  }
}

/// [package] as a plan of the app, with its intro offer if its product is
/// among [withIntro].
Plan _planOf(Package package, Set<String> withIntro) {
  final product = package.storeProduct;
  final intro = product.introductoryPrice;
  return Plan(
    id: package.identifier,
    title: _titleOf(product),
    price: product.priceString,
    amount: product.price,
    currencyCode: product.currencyCode,
    period: _periodIn(product.subscriptionPeriod),
    introOffer: intro == null || !withIntro.contains(product.identifier)
        ? null
        : _introOf(intro),
  );
}

/// The title of [product]. Google Play appends the name of the app in
/// parentheses to the title of a product, which the app leaves out.
String _titleOf(StoreProduct product) {
  if (defaultTargetPlatform != TargetPlatform.android) return product.title;
  final title = product.title.replaceFirst(RegExp(r'\s*\([^()]*\)$'), '');
  return title.isEmpty ? product.title : title;
}

/// [intro] as an intro offer of a plan, or `null` when RevenueCat gives it
/// no period that the app knows.
IntroOffer? _introOf(IntroductoryPrice intro) {
  final unit = _units[intro.periodUnit];
  if (unit == null || intro.periodNumberOfUnits < 1 || intro.cycles < 1) {
    return null;
  }
  return IntroOffer(
    price: intro.priceString,
    amount: intro.price,
    period: BillingPeriod(intro.periodNumberOfUnits, unit),
    cycles: intro.cycles,
  );
}

/// The units of the periods of RevenueCat.
const _units = {
  PeriodUnit.day: BillingUnit.day,
  PeriodUnit.week: BillingUnit.week,
  PeriodUnit.month: BillingUnit.month,
  PeriodUnit.year: BillingUnit.year,
};

/// The period of a subscription in ISO 8601, such as `P1M`, or `null` for a
/// product that the user pays for once.
BillingPeriod? _periodIn(String? iso) {
  final match = RegExp(r'^P(\d+)([DWMY])$').firstMatch(iso ?? '');
  if (match == null) return null;
  final count = int.parse(match[1]!);
  if (count < 1) return null;
  return BillingPeriod(count, switch (match[2]!) {
    'D' => BillingUnit.day,
    'W' => BillingUnit.week,
    'M' => BillingUnit.month,
    _ => BillingUnit.year,
  });
}

/// The code of RevenueCat in [error], or `null` when it is no code of
/// RevenueCat, as for an error of the plugin itself, whose code is no
/// number.
PurchasesErrorCode? _codeOf(PlatformException error) {
  final number = int.tryParse(error.code);
  if (number == null ||
      number < 0 ||
      number >= PurchasesErrorCode.values.length) {
    return null;
  }
  return PurchasesErrorCode.values[number];
}

/// Whether [error] tells that the user cancelled a purchase.
bool _cancelled(PlatformException error) {
  final details = error.details;
  return _codeOf(error) == PurchasesErrorCode.purchaseCancelledError ||
      (details is Map && details['userCancelled'] == true);
}

/// [error] of RevenueCat, configured with the key [keyName] of `.env`, as a
/// failure of the purchases of the app.
PurchasesFailure _failureOf(PlatformException error, String? keyName) {
  final code = _codeOf(error);
  final reason = switch (code) {
    PurchasesErrorCode.paymentPendingError => PurchasesFailureReason.pending,
    PurchasesErrorCode.networkError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.offlineConnectionError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.productRequestTimeout ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.apiEndpointBlocked => PurchasesFailureReason.network,
    PurchasesErrorCode.productAlreadyPurchasedError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.receiptAlreadyInUseError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.receiptInUseByOtherSubscriberError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.purchaseBelongsToOtherUser =>
      PurchasesFailureReason.alreadyOwned,
    PurchasesErrorCode.purchaseNotAllowedError =>
      PurchasesFailureReason.notAllowed,
    PurchasesErrorCode.productNotAvailableForPurchaseError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.purchaseInvalidError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.ineligibleError => PurchasesFailureReason.unavailable,
    PurchasesErrorCode.configurationError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.invalidCredentialsError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.invalidAppleSubscriptionKeyError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.insufficientPermissionsError =>
      PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured,
    PurchasesErrorCode.storeProblemError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.invalidReceiptError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.missingReceiptFileError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.unexpectedBackendResponseError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.unknownBackendError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.customerInfoError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.systemInfoError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.signatureVerificationFailed ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.testStoreSimulatedPurchaseError =>
      PurchasesFailureReason.storeProblem,
    _ => PurchasesFailureReason.unknown,
  };
  return PurchasesFailure(reason, developerHint: _hintOf(error, code, keyName));
}

/// What [error] with [code] tells the developer of the app, whose app
/// configured RevenueCat with the key [keyName] of `.env`.
String _hintOf(
  PlatformException error,
  PurchasesErrorCode? code,
  String? keyName,
) {
  final details = error.details;
  final underlying = details is Map ? details['underlyingErrorMessage'] : null;
  final said = [
    '${code?.name ?? 'code ${error.code}'}: ${error.message}',
    if (underlying is String && underlying.isNotEmpty) underlying,
  ].join(' ');
  return switch (code) {
    PurchasesErrorCode.invalidCredentialsError =>
      'RevenueCat refused $keyName of .env ($said): copy the public API key '
          'of the app in RevenueCat into .env.',
    PurchasesErrorCode.productAlreadyPurchasedError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.receiptAlreadyInUseError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.receiptInUseByOtherSubscriberError ||
    PurchasesErrorCode.purchaseBelongsToOtherUser =>
      'The store account owns the product already ($said).',
    PurchasesErrorCode.testStoreSimulatedPurchaseError =>
      'The Test Store of RevenueCat failed the purchase, as its dialog was '
          'told to ($said).',
    _ => said,
  };
}
=== lib/core/push/app_push.dart (role:push) ===
import 'dart:async';
import 'dart:convert';

import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';
import 'package:my_app/core/push/firebase_push_service.dart' as impl0;

import '../notifications/app_notifications.dart';
import 'push_service.dart';

export 'push_service.dart' show PushMessage;

/// The push messages of the app: the address of the device, the messages
/// that arrive while the app is in the foreground, and the taps of the user
/// on them.
final appPush = AppPushController._();

/// Receives the push messages of the app through the service of the
/// provider of push messages. The app has one, [appPush].
///
/// It shows a message with a title or a body that arrives while the app is
/// in the foreground as a notification of the app, through
/// [appNotifications], where the platform does not show it itself. Such a
/// notification has [appPush] as its owner, so a tap on it reaches [taps]
/// and not [NotificationsController.taps].
///
/// An error of the service on its streams, of a notification that the
/// controller shows, of an address that it asks for, or of the message that
/// launched the app, has no caller, so it reaches the handlers of the
/// uncaught errors of the app, and the controller goes on.
final class AppPushController {
  AppPushController._();

  /// The owner of the notifications that show the messages, under
  /// [notificationOwnerKey] in their data.
  static const _owner = 'push';

  /// The key under which such a notification keeps its message in its data.
  static const _messageKey = 'push.message';

  PushService? _service;
  StreamSubscription<PushMessage>? _serviceMessages;
  StreamSubscription<PushMessage>? _serviceTaps;
  StreamSubscription<String>? _serviceAddresses;
  StreamSubscription<NotificationTap>? _notificationTaps;
  PushMessage? _launchMessage;

  /// The service whose address the controller is asking for, if any.
  PushService? _asking;

  /// Tells when the user comes back to the app.
  _ResumeObserver? _observer;

  final ValueNotifier<String?> _address = ValueNotifier(null);

  final StreamController<PushMessage> _messages =
      StreamController<PushMessage>.broadcast();

  final StreamController<PushMessage> _taps =
      StreamController<PushMessage>.broadcast();

  /// The address of the device, to which the backend of the app sends the
  /// messages for it, or `null` while the platform gives none. Send each new
  /// one to the backend: it may come late, such as on iOS, and it may
  /// change. A widget follows it with a `ValueListenableBuilder`.
  ///
  /// The controller asks for it when it starts, without waiting for it, and
  /// again each time the user comes back to the app while it has none.
  ValueListenable<String?> get address => _address;

  /// The message whose tap launched the app, or `null` if the app was
  /// launched in another way: known once `bootstrap()` is over. The message
  /// is not among the [taps].
  PushMessage? get launchMessage => _launchMessage;

  /// Tells of each message that arrives while the app is in the foreground:
  /// a broadcast stream, the same for the life of the app.
  late final Stream<PushMessage> messages = _messages.stream;

  /// Tells of each tap of the user on a message while the app runs, whether
  /// the platform or the app showed it: a broadcast stream, the same for the
  /// life of the app.
  late final Stream<PushMessage> taps = _taps.stream;

  /// Starts with [service]: follows its messages, its taps and its address,
  /// asks for the address without waiting for it, and takes the message
  /// whose tap launched the app.
  ///
  /// `initPush()` calls it in `bootstrap()`, after the notifications of the
  /// app started, so [launchMessage] is known before the first frame. It may
  /// be called again, with the service that the app has from then on:
  /// `initPush()` does so in a test of the next launch of the app, and a
  /// test gives it a service of its own. The address is then that of the
  /// new service, and `null` until it gives one.
  ///
  /// If the service fails to give the message that launched the app, the
  /// app still starts: the error reaches the handlers of the uncaught errors
  /// of the app, and [launchMessage] is the message of a notification that
  /// the app showed, if its tap launched the app, or `null`.
  Future<void> start(PushService service) async {
    unawaited(_serviceMessages?.cancel());
    unawaited(_serviceTaps?.cancel());
    unawaited(_serviceAddresses?.cancel());
    _service = service;
    _launchMessage = null;
    _address.value = null;
    // An error of these streams has no caller: it reaches the handlers of
    // the uncaught errors of the app, and the streams go on.
    _serviceMessages = service.messages.listen(_onMessage);
    _serviceTaps = service.taps.listen(_taps.add);
    _serviceAddresses = service.addressChanges.listen(_setAddress);
    _notificationTaps ??= appNotifications
        .tapsOf(_owner)
        .listen((tap) => _taps.add(_messageIn(tap.data)));
    if (_observer == null) {
      final observer = _observer = _ResumeObserver(_askForAddress);
      WidgetsBinding.instance.addObserver(observer);
    }
    _askForAddress();
    PushMessage? launched;
    try {
      launched = await service.launchMessage();
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      // A provider that fails here would otherwise keep the app from
      // starting.
      Zone.current.handleUncaughtError(error, stackTrace);
    }
    final tap = appNotifications.launchTapOf(_owner);
    _launchMessage = launched ?? (tap == null ? null : _messageIn(tap.data));
  }

  /// Asks the service for the address while the controller has none, unless
  /// it is asking already.
  void _askForAddress() {
    final service = _service!;
    if (_address.value != null || identical(_asking, service)) return;
    _asking = service;
    unawaited(_answer(service));
  }

  Future<void> _answer(PushService service) async {
    try {
      final address = await service.address();
      // An answer of a service that the controller no longer has, or one
      // after the service told of an address, changes nothing.
      if (address != null &&
          identical(service, _service) &&
          _address.value == null) {
        _setAddress(address);
      }
    } finally {
      if (identical(_asking, service)) _asking = null;
    }
  }

  void _setAddress(String address) {
    if (address == _address.value) return;
    _address.value = address;
    if (kDebugMode) {
      debugPrint('The address of this device for push messages: $address');
    }
  }

  /// Tells of [message], and shows it as a notification of the app if the
  /// platform does not show it and it has a title or a body. An empty title
  /// or body counts as none.
  void _onMessage(PushMessage message) {
    _messages.add(message);
    final title = _textOf(message.title);
    final body = _textOf(message.body);
    final text = title ?? body;
    if (text == null || _service!.platformShowsInForeground) return;
    final json = jsonEncode({
      'id': message.id,
      'title': message.title,
      'body': message.body,
      'data': message.data,
    });
    unawaited(
      appNotifications.show(
        AppNotification(
          // An equal message takes the same id, so its notification takes
          // the place of the one that showed it before rather than adding
          // another, and a negative one, so it never takes the place of a
          // notification of the app.
          id: -1 - (json.hashCode & 0x7FFFFFFF),
          // A message with a body alone shows it as its title.
          title: text,
          body: title == null ? null : body,
          data: {
            ...message.data,
            notificationOwnerKey: _owner,
            _messageKey: json,
          },
        ),
      ),
    );
  }

  /// [text], or `null` if it is empty.
  static String? _textOf(String? text) =>
      text == null || text.isEmpty ? null : text;

  /// The message that [data], the data of a notification that showed a
  /// message, keeps under [_messageKey], or a message with the rest of the
  /// data if the notification lost it.
  static PushMessage _messageIn(Map<String, String> data) =>
      switch (_decoded(data[_messageKey])) {
        {
          'id': final String? id,
          'title': final String? title,
          'body': final String? body,
          'data': final Map<String, Object?> values,
        }
            when values.values.every((value) => value is String) =>
          PushMessage(
            id: id,
            title: title,
            body: body,
            data: Map<String, String>.from(values),
          ),
        _ => PushMessage(
          data: {
            for (final MapEntry(:key, :value) in data.entries)
              if (key != notificationOwnerKey && key != _messageKey) key: value,
          },
        ),
      };

  /// What the JSON [text] holds, or `null` for no text or one that is no
  /// JSON.
  static Object? _decoded(String? text) {
    if (text == null) return null;
    try {
      return jsonDecode(text);
    } on FormatException {
      return null;
    }
  }
}

/// Calls [_onResume] each time the app is resumed, whatever state it was in
/// before.
final class _ResumeObserver with WidgetsBindingObserver {
  _ResumeObserver(this._onResume);

  final VoidCallback _onResume;

  @override
  void didChangeAppLifecycleState(AppLifecycleState state) {
    if (state == AppLifecycleState.resumed) _onResume();
  }
}

/// Returns the service of the provider of push messages, which [initPush]
/// created: `bootstrap()` awaits it before the first frame, and there is
/// none to return before that.
///
/// The code of the app does not call it: it uses [appPush].
PushService createPushService() => _pushService;

late PushService _pushService;

/// Creates the service of the provider of push messages and starts
/// [appPush] with it; `bootstrap()` awaits it before the first frame, after
/// the notifications of the app started, and nothing else in the app calls
/// it.
///
/// It may run again, as a test does to see what the next launch of the app
/// finds: it creates the service anew, with what the device kept, and
/// starts [appPush] with it.
Future<void> initPush() async {
  _pushService = impl0.createFirebasePushService();
  await appPush.start(_pushService);
}
=== lib/core/push/firebase_push_service.dart (firebase_messaging) ===
import 'dart:async';
import 'dart:convert';

import 'package:firebase_core/firebase_core.dart' show FirebaseException;
import 'package:firebase_messaging/firebase_messaging.dart';
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

import 'push_service.dart';

/// Returns the push messages of the app on Firebase Cloud Messaging. It asks
/// the user nothing.
///
/// Firebase is initialized first, as `bootstrap()` does. On iOS it sets the
/// presentation options of a notification that arrives while the app is in
/// the foreground to an alert, a badge and a sound. firebase_messaging
/// answers iOS for every such notification, the local notifications of the
/// app too, with those options: without them, iOS would show none of them.
PushService createFirebasePushService() {
  final service = FirebasePushService(FirebaseMessaging.instance);
  if (service.platformShowsInForeground) {
    unawaited(
      FirebaseMessaging.instance.setForegroundNotificationPresentationOptions(
        alert: true,
        badge: true,
        sound: true,
      ),
    );
  }
  return service;
}

/// [message] as a push message of the app: its id, the title and the body
/// of its notification, and its data, with a value that is no string as its
/// JSON.
PushMessage _messageOf(RemoteMessage message) => PushMessage(
  id: message.messageId,
  title: message.notification?.title,
  body: message.notification?.body,
  data: {
    for (final MapEntry(:key, :value) in message.data.entries)
      key: value is String ? value : jsonEncode(value),
  },
);

/// How long the service waits before it asks iOS for the token of the Apple
/// Push Notification service the first time: twice as long each next time,
/// up to [_longestWait], until it has waited [_longestWatch] in all.
const _firstWait = Duration(milliseconds: 500);
const _longestWait = Duration(seconds: 8);
const _longestWatch = Duration(minutes: 2);

/// The push messages of the app on
/// [Firebase Cloud Messaging](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging).
///
/// The code of the app does not use it: it receives push messages through
/// `appPush` of `app_push.dart`.
///
/// The address of the device is the registration token of Firebase Cloud
/// Messaging. On iOS it needs the token of the Apple Push Notification
/// service, which may come a while after the app started, or never, as on a
/// build that is not signed for push messages: [address] is `null` until
/// then. When that token comes, firebase_messaging tells the app of no
/// registration token. So while something listens to [addressChanges], the
/// service asks iOS for the token now and then, which needs no network,
/// for at most two minutes, and tells of the registration token once iOS
/// has the token. [addressChanges] also tells of each new token that
/// Firebase gives the device.
///
/// On Android, the system shows a message that arrives in the background,
/// in the channel and with the icon that the manifest of the app names, and
/// the app shows one that arrives in the foreground. On iOS, the system
/// shows both, with the presentation options of
/// [createFirebasePushService].
final class FirebasePushService implements PushService {
  /// Creates the service on [messaging].
  ///
  /// It reads the stream of the new tokens at once, which sets up the
  /// plugin, so that the messages and the taps that the platform tells of
  /// from then on reach the streams of the service.
  FirebasePushService(this._messaging) : _tokens = _messaging.onTokenRefresh;

  final FirebaseMessaging _messaging;

  /// The new tokens that Firebase tells of.
  final Stream<String> _tokens;

  late final StreamController<String> _addresses =
      StreamController<String>.broadcast(
        onListen: _startListening,
        onCancel: _stopListening,
      );

  StreamSubscription<String>? _tokenSubscription;

  /// Whether the service waits for the token of the Apple Push Notification
  /// service, since [address] failed for want of it.
  bool _awaitingApnsToken = false;

  /// The timer of the next time the service asks iOS for that token.
  Timer? _nextCheck;

  /// How long the service waits before it asks next time, and how long it
  /// has waited for that token so far.
  Duration _wait = _firstWait;
  Duration _waited = Duration.zero;

  @override
  bool get platformShowsInForeground =>
      defaultTargetPlatform == TargetPlatform.iOS;

  @override
  Stream<String> get addressChanges => _addresses.stream;

  @override
  final Stream<PushMessage> messages = FirebaseMessaging.onMessage.map(
    _messageOf,
  );

  @override
  final Stream<PushMessage> taps = FirebaseMessaging.onMessageOpenedApp.map(
    _messageOf,
  );

  @override
  Future<String?> address() async {
    try {
      return await _messaging.getToken();
    } on FirebaseException catch (error) {
      _printWithoutAddress(error);
      // The plugin gives no token on iOS before the device has the token of
      // the Apple Push Notification service.
      if (error.code == 'apns-token-not-set') _awaitApnsToken();
      return null;
    } on Object catch (error) {
      // Android has no token without Google Play services or a network.
      _printWithoutAddress(error);
      return null;
    }
  }

  @override
  Future<PushMessage?> launchMessage() async {
    try {
      final message = await _messaging.getInitialMessage();
      return message == null ? null : _messageOf(message);
    } on Object catch (error) {
      if (kDebugMode) {
        debugPrint('Firebase Cloud Messaging has no launch message: $error');
      }
      return null;
    }
  }

  static void _printWithoutAddress(Object error) {
    if (kDebugMode) {
      debugPrint('Firebase Cloud Messaging has no address yet: $error');
    }
  }

  void _startListening() {
    _tokenSubscription = _tokens.listen(_tellAddress);
    if (_awaitingApnsToken) _scheduleCheck();
  }

  /// No timer of the service outlives the last listener.
  Future<void> _stopListening() async {
    _nextCheck?.cancel();
    _nextCheck = null;
    await _tokenSubscription?.cancel();
    _tokenSubscription = null;
  }

  void _tellAddress(String address) {
    _awaitingApnsToken = false;
    _nextCheck?.cancel();
    _nextCheck = null;
    _addresses.add(address);
  }

  void _awaitApnsToken() {
    if (_awaitingApnsToken) return;
    _awaitingApnsToken = true;
    _wait = _firstWait;
    _waited = Duration.zero;
    if (_addresses.hasListener) _scheduleCheck();
  }

  void _scheduleCheck() {
    if (_nextCheck != null) return;
    if (_waited >= _longestWatch) {
      _awaitingApnsToken = false;
      return;
    }
    final wait = _wait;
    _waited += wait;
    _wait = wait * 2 > _longestWait ? _longestWait : wait * 2;
    _nextCheck = Timer(wait, () => unawaited(_checkApnsToken()));
  }

  /// Asks iOS for the token of the Apple Push Notification service, and
  /// once it has one, Firebase for the registration token, which it tells
  /// of.
  Future<void> _checkApnsToken() async {
    _nextCheck = null;
    String? apnsToken;
    try {
      apnsToken = await _messaging.getAPNSToken();
    } on Object {
      apnsToken = null;
    }
    if (!_awaitingApnsToken || !_addresses.hasListener) return;
    if (apnsToken == null) return _scheduleCheck();
    _awaitingApnsToken = false;
    try {
      final address = await _messaging.getToken();
      if (address != null && _addresses.hasListener) _addresses.add(address);
    } on Object catch (error) {
      _printWithoutAddress(error);
    }
  }
}
=== lib/core/push/push_service.dart (role:push) ===
/// A push message: a message that a server sends to this device through the
/// provider of push messages.
final class PushMessage {
  /// Creates the message with its [id], [title], [body] and [data], each if
  /// it has one.
  const PushMessage({this.id, this.title, this.body, this.data = const {}});

  /// The id that the provider gives the message, or `null` if it gives none.
  final String? id;

  /// The title of the message, or `null` for a message without one.
  final String? title;

  /// The text of the message, or `null` for a message without one.
  final String? body;

  /// What the server sent for the app to read, such as the screen that a
  /// tap opens. A value that the platform gives as something other than a
  /// string is its JSON.
  final Map<String, String> data;
}

/// The push messages of the platform, which the provider of push messages
/// implements: the address of the device, the messages that arrive while
/// the app is in the foreground, and the taps of the user on the messages
/// that the platform shows.
///
/// The code of the app does not use it: it uses `appPush` of
/// `app_push.dart`, which calls this service, shows a message in the
/// foreground where the platform does not, and tells of the taps on the
/// messages that it showed.
///
/// Every implementation keeps to this:
/// - The function that creates the service asks the user nothing. It may be
///   called again, and the service that it returns then has the address
///   that the device kept.
/// - The service never asks for the permission to show notifications, which
///   the notifications of the app ask for, and never keeps the notifications
///   of the app from showing while the app is in the foreground.
/// - [address] completes with the address of the device, or with `null`
///   while the platform gives none, and does not throw. It needs no
///   permission. The address stays the same from one launch of the app to
///   the next, unless the platform gives the device another, which
///   [addressChanges] tells of.
/// - [addressChanges] tells of each new address, and of the first address
///   that the device gets after [address] completed with `null`, such as
///   one that comes late on iOS.
/// - [messages] tells of each message that arrives while the app is in the
///   foreground, once.
/// - A value of the data of a message that the platform gives as something
///   other than a string becomes its JSON, as `jsonEncode` writes it.
/// - A message with a title or a body that arrives while the app is in the
///   background, or closed, is shown by the platform, on Android in the
///   channel of the notifications of the app. [taps] tells of each tap on
///   such a message while the app runs, and [launchMessage] is the message
///   whose tap launched the app, which is not among the [taps].
/// - In the foreground, the platform or the provider shows such a message
///   where [platformShowsInForeground] is true, and [taps] then tells of a
///   tap on it too. Where it is false, the service shows nothing of the
///   message, and the app shows it as a notification of its own.
/// - [launchMessage] completes without the network, and does not throw.
abstract interface class PushService {
  /// Whether the platform or the provider shows a message with a title or a
  /// body that arrives while the app is in the foreground. The app shows it
  /// otherwise.
  bool get platformShowsInForeground;

  /// The address of the device, to which a server sends the messages for
  /// this app, or `null` while the platform gives none.
  Future<String?> address();

  /// Tells of each new address of the device, and of the first one after
  /// [address] gave none: a broadcast stream.
  Stream<String> get addressChanges;

  /// Tells of each message that arrives while the app is in the foreground:
  /// a broadcast stream.
  Stream<PushMessage> get messages;

  /// Tells of each tap of the user on a message that the platform showed,
  /// while the app runs: a broadcast stream. The tap that launched the app
  /// is not among them.
  Stream<PushMessage> get taps;

  /// The message whose tap launched the app, or `null` if the app was
  /// launched in another way.
  Future<PushMessage?> launchMessage();
}
=== lib/core/router/app_router.dart (role:router) ===
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';
import 'package:my_app/features/onboarding/onboarding_status.dart' as guard0;
import 'package:my_app/features/paywall/paywall_guards.dart' as guard2;
import 'package:my_app/features/sign_in/sign_in_guards.dart' as guard1;

import 'app_router_factory.dart';
import 'navigation.dart';

/// The router of the app, created on first use.
final AppRouter appRouter = createAppRouter();

/// The router of the app: its configuration for `MaterialApp.router` and the
/// navigation that screens use through `context.nav`.
abstract interface class AppRouter {
  /// The configuration that `MaterialApp.router` takes as `routerConfig`.
  RouterConfig<Object> get config;

  /// The navigator that [context] navigates with.
  AppNavigator navigatorOf(BuildContext context);
}

/// Moves between the locations of the app.
abstract interface class AppNavigator {
  /// Shows [location] with the chain of its parents below it as the stack.
  ///
  /// In the main navigation, it selects the branch of [location] and makes
  /// the chain its stack; for a location outside the main navigation, the
  /// chain replaces the whole stack, main navigation included.
  void go(AppLocation location);

  /// Shows [location] on top of the current stack and completes with the
  /// value the page returns when it closes.
  ///
  /// In the main navigation, the current stack is that of the selected
  /// branch, whichever branch [location] belongs to, and the selected
  /// destination stays; a location outside the main navigation is shown
  /// over it. A location in the main navigation goes only on top of the
  /// main navigation itself: from a page shown over it, this throws a
  /// [StateError] and leaves the stack as it is, so [go] to the location
  /// instead. From a page with no main navigation below it, the main
  /// navigation comes on top with the location.
  Future<T?> push<T extends Object?>(AppLocation location);

  /// Replaces the top of the current stack with [location].
  ///
  /// As with [push], a location in the main navigation cannot replace a
  /// page shown over the main navigation: this throws a [StateError] and
  /// leaves the stack as it is, so [go] to the location instead.
  void replace(AppLocation location);
}

/// A guard of the routes of the app. Without [routes] it is a gate: while
/// it does not allow, the router shows its target in place of every
/// location outside its flow. With [routes] it keeps the user only from
/// those routes, and the router shows every other route as it is.
///
/// While a gate does not allow, `go()`, `push()` and `replace()` of a
/// location that it keeps the user from show its target in place of the
/// whole stack, and `push()` completes with `null`. Once it allows, the
/// router shows the location that the user last asked for, or the one that
/// the gate took the user from if it [resumes], or else the screen that
/// the app starts on.
///
/// While a guard with [routes] does not allow, a request for one of them
/// opens its target over the page that the user is on, and the request
/// waits. Back returns to that page and drops the request. Once the guard
/// allows, the router closes the pages of the flow and does what was asked:
/// `push()` shows the location over the page that the user was on, and
/// completes with the value of its page, `go()` shows it in place of the
/// stack, and `replace()` in place of that page. A further request for one
/// of the [routes] does nothing while the flow is open. A location from
/// the platform opens the target over the screen that the app starts on.
/// When the guard stops allowing, the router closes each page of its
/// [routes] and the pages over it.
///
/// The routes of its flow show only while it does not allow, or while
/// another guard with the same flow does not. At any other time the router
/// shows the screen that the app starts on in place of a location of the
/// flow (see [flowIsOver]), or the target of a gate while that one does
/// not allow. So the code of the app changes what [allows] reads. It
/// navigates into a flow only while a guard with [routes] and that flow
/// does not allow and every gate does, as to a sign-in that a guest opens.
final class RouteGuard {
  /// Creates the guard [name].
  const RouteGuard(
    this.name, {
    required this.allows,
    required this.redirectTo,
    required this.flow,
    this.resumes = true,
    this.routes,
  });

  /// The full name of the guard: its module and its name there.
  final String name;

  /// Whether the guard lets the user see the locations that it keeps them
  /// from otherwise; it notifies its listeners when that changes.
  final ValueListenable<bool> allows;

  /// The target of the guard: the location that the router shows while the
  /// guard does not allow.
  final AppLocation redirectTo;

  /// The full names of the routes that the user may see while the guard
  /// does not allow: the route of [redirectTo] and the routes below it.
  /// Once every guard with this flow allows, the router shows none of them.
  final Set<String> flow;

  /// Whether the router brings the user back to the location that the
  /// guard takes them from when it stops allowing, once it allows again.
  ///
  /// With `false`, the router forgets what it remembers when the guard
  /// stops allowing: where the user was, whichever gate took them from
  /// it, and a location that was asked for before. So once the gates
  /// allow, the user comes to the screen that the app starts on, as the
  /// next user does after a sign-out, unless a location was asked for
  /// since the guard stopped, such as a link.
  ///
  /// The router remembers a location only for a gate. A guard with
  /// [routes] closes its pages when it stops allowing, so the user is on
  /// the page below them, whatever this says. With `false`, such a guard
  /// still makes the router forget what a gate made it remember.
  final bool resumes;

  /// The full names of the routes that the guard keeps the user from while
  /// it does not allow, or `null` for a gate, which keeps the user from
  /// every route outside its [flow].
  ///
  /// A guard with routes stands for something that only those routes need,
  /// such as an account: every other route shows whatever the guard says.
  /// None of its routes is in the [flow] of a guard. To ask for it on one
  /// more route, add the full name of the route here, and that of each
  /// route below it: a route does not ask because the route above it does.
  final Set<String>? routes;
}

/// The guards of the routes of the app, in the order the app asks them:
/// the gates first, and then the guards with [RouteGuard.routes].
final List<RouteGuard> routeGuards = [
  RouteGuard(
    'onboarding.firstRun',
    allows: guard0.onboardingCompleted(),
    redirectTo: const OnboardingOnboardingLocation(),
    flow: const {'onboarding.onboarding'},
  ),
  RouteGuard(
    'sign_in.gate',
    allows: guard1.signInAllowsApp(),
    redirectTo: const SignInSignInLocation(),
    flow: const {'sign_in.signIn', 'sign_in.signUp', 'sign_in.resetPassword'},
    resumes: false,
  ),
  RouteGuard(
    'sign_in.hasAccount',
    allows: guard1.signInHasAccount(),
    redirectTo: const SignInSignInLocation(),
    flow: const {'sign_in.signIn', 'sign_in.signUp', 'sign_in.resetPassword'},
    resumes: false,
    routes: const {'sign_in.account'},
  ),
  RouteGuard(
    'paywall.hasPremium',
    allows: guard2.paywallHasPremium(),
    redirectTo: const PaywallPaywallLocation(),
    flow: const {'paywall.paywall'},
    routes: const {'paywall.subscription'},
  ),
];

/// The location that the router shows when it is asked for the route
/// [routeName] and a guard keeps the user from it, or `null` if the guards
/// let the user see the route.
///
/// [routeName] is the full name of a route, or `null` for a screen that is
/// no route of a module, such as the error screen of the router. The first
/// gate that does not allow decides, and no guard after it is asked: it
/// shows its target in place of every route outside its flow. When every
/// gate allows, the first guard that has the route among its
/// [RouteGuard.routes] and does not allow shows its target: over the page
/// that the user is on, not in place of it.
///
/// A route that the guards let the user see may still be in a flow that is
/// over, in place of which the router shows the screen that the app starts
/// on; see [flowIsOver].
AppLocation? redirectOf(String? routeName) =>
    _guardKeepingFrom(routeName)?.redirectTo;

/// The guard that keeps the user from the route [routeName], or `null` if
/// the guards let the user see it: the first gate that does not allow,
/// unless the route is in its flow, or else the first guard with
/// [RouteGuard.routes] that does not allow and has the route among them.
RouteGuard? _guardKeepingFrom(String? routeName) {
  for (final guard in routeGuards) {
    if (guard.allows.value) continue;
    final routes = guard.routes;
    if (routes == null) return guard.flow.contains(routeName) ? null : guard;
    if (routes.contains(routeName)) return guard;
  }
  return null;
}

/// Whether the route [routeName] is in the flow of a guard and every guard
/// with that flow allows: the flow is over. The router then shows the
/// screen that the app starts on in place of the route, unless another
/// guard does not allow and shows its target instead (see
/// [redirectOf]).
///
/// [routeName] is the full name of a route, or `null` for a screen that is
/// no route of a module, which is in no flow. Two guards with one target
/// have one flow, which is over once both allow.
bool flowIsOver(String? routeName) {
  final guards = routeGuards.where((guard) => guard.flow.contains(routeName));
  return guards.isNotEmpty && guards.every((guard) => guard.allows.value);
}

/// Notifies its listeners when a guard starts or stops allowing, so that
/// the router tells its [GuardedNavigation] of its pages.
final Listenable guardChanges = Listenable.merge([
  for (final guard in routeGuards) guard.allows,
]);

/// What [GuardedNavigation.asked] answers a router that is not to show the
/// location that it asked about: [ShowInstead], [ShowOver] or [ShowNothing].
sealed class WhenAsked<L> {}

/// What [GuardedNavigation.changed] answers a router that is to change its
/// pages: [ShowInstead] or [ClosePages].
sealed class WhenChanged<L> {}

/// The router shows [location] in place of its whole stack, as `go()` to
/// it does. A `push()` that it asked about completes with `null`.
final class ShowInstead<L> implements WhenAsked<L>, WhenChanged<L> {
  /// Creates the answer.
  const ShowInstead(this.location);

  /// The location to show: the target of a gate, the location that the
  /// user comes back to, or the screen that the app starts on.
  final L location;
}

/// The router shows [location] over the page on top, as `push()` of it
/// does, and keeps the request that it asked about waiting while a page of
/// a route of [flow] is among its pages.
///
/// The router makes the request again once [GuardedNavigation.changed] closes
/// the last of those pages, unless that answer drops the request. And it
/// drops the request when the last of them leaves in another way, as when
/// the user goes back. It keeps one request: a request that waited before
/// is dropped.
final class ShowOver<L> implements WhenAsked<L> {
  /// Creates the answer.
  const ShowOver(this.location, {required this.flow});

  /// The location to show on top: the target of a guard with
  /// [RouteGuard.routes], or a page of its flow that was asked for with no
  /// pages.
  final L location;

  /// The full names of the routes of the flow of that guard.
  final Set<String> flow;
}

/// The router shows nothing and leaves its pages as they are: the flow of
/// the guard is open already. A `push()` that it asked about completes
/// with `null`.
final class ShowNothing<L> implements WhenAsked<L> {
  /// Creates the answer.
  const ShowNothing();
}

/// The router closes the [pages] pages on top, at once and whatever its
/// navigator shows over them, such as a dialog. Each `push()` that showed
/// one of them completes with `null`, at once or when the frame is over.
///
/// Then, if a request waits: with [dropsRequest] the router drops it. And
/// else it makes the request again, if no page of the flow of that request
/// is left.
final class ClosePages<L> implements WhenChanged<L> {
  /// Creates the answer.
  const ClosePages(this.pages, {required this.dropsRequest});

  /// How many pages to close, from the one on top down. The router said of
  /// each that it can close it on its own, and a page stays below them.
  final int pages;

  /// Whether the page that the last flow was opened over is among those
  /// that close: the request that waits for that flow was made on that
  /// page, so it is not made again on the page below.
  final bool dropsRequest;
}

/// The guards of the routes as the router of the app asks them, with what
/// the gates make it remember: the location that the user comes back to
/// once the gates allow it.
///
/// [L] is how the router knows a location that it can show as `go()`
/// does, such as its URI. The router asks [asked] once for each request to
/// show a location, before it shows it, and tells [changed] of its pages
/// each time [guardChanges] notifies. It does what either answers, and shows
/// the location, or leaves its pages as they are, when the answer is
/// `null`.
///
/// A gate answers with a location that takes the place of the whole stack.
/// A guard with [RouteGuard.routes] opens its target over the page that
/// the user is on and closes it again, so the user can go back from it,
/// and the request that opened it waits with the router, not here.
final class GuardedNavigation<L> {
  /// Creates the guards for a router whose location `/`, the screen that
  /// the app starts on, is [start], and which knows a location of the
  /// navigation, such as the target of a guard, as [locationOf] says.
  GuardedNavigation({required this.start, required this.locationOf});

  /// The location `/` of the router: the screen that the app starts on.
  final L start;

  /// The location of the router for a location of the navigation.
  final L Function(AppLocation location) locationOf;

  /// The location that the user comes back to once the gates allow it,
  /// with the full name of its route, or `null` if there is none.
  ({String? route, L location})? _remembered;

  /// The guards that do not bring the user back (see [RouteGuard.resumes])
  /// and that allowed when the class last looked at the guards, which it
  /// does each time it is asked or told. One of them that does not allow
  /// at the next look has stopped allowing. The class first looks when it
  /// is first asked or told, so a guard that does not allow then has not
  /// stopped.
  late final Set<RouteGuard> _allowed = {
    for (final guard in routeGuards)
      if (!guard.resumes && guard.allows.value) guard,
  };

  /// How many pages the flow that [asked] opened last was opened over: the
  /// request that waits for that flow was made on the one on top of them.
  int _below = 0;

  /// What the router does in place of showing [location], whose route has
  /// the full name [route], or `null` to show it.
  ///
  /// The router asks about a location before it shows it: the one the app
  /// starts on, each one that `go()`, `push()` or `replace()` is asked to
  /// show, and each one from the platform.
  /// [onTopOf] are the full names of the routes of the pages that the user
  /// can get back to then, as the router tells [changed] of them. The
  /// router gives none when it has no page yet, and none for a location
  /// from the platform, which takes the place of the stack.
  ///
  /// The answer is the first of these:
  /// - [ShowInstead] of the target of the gate that keeps the user from the
  ///   location (see [redirectOf]). The location is then remembered in place of
  ///   the one before it, so the user comes back to the latest one that
  ///   they or the platform asked for, whether or not that gate brings the
  ///   user back (see [RouteGuard.resumes]). A location in the flow of a
  ///   guard is never remembered: once that guard allows, its flow is over.
  /// - [ShowInstead] of [start] for a location in a flow that is over (see
  ///   [flowIsOver]).
  /// - for a location that a guard with [RouteGuard.routes] keeps the user
  ///   from: [ShowNothing] while a page of the flow of that guard is among
  ///   [onTopOf], on top or below another page, since the flow is open
  ///   already; and else [ShowOver] of the target of the guard, with its
  ///   flow.
  /// - for a location that no guard keeps the user from and that is asked
  ///   for with no pages, in a flow that only guards with
  ///   [RouteGuard.routes] keep open: [ShowOver] of the location itself,
  ///   with that flow. So a page of such a flow never stands alone when a
  ///   link asks for it: the router shows it over [start], and back leads
  ///   into the app. The request that waits is the page itself: asked
  ///   about again once the flow closes, it is answered with [start],
  ///   where the user is then. A flow that a gate keeps open too is the
  ///   gate's, and takes the place of the stack.
  ///
  /// For a guard with routes the class remembers no location, only over
  /// how many pages its flow was opened, for [changed]. So the answer
  /// depends on [onTopOf] and not on what the class answered before. Asked
  /// again about a location that it answered [ShowOver] for, with a page of
  /// the flow among [onTopOf], it answers [ShowNothing] for a location that
  /// the guard keeps the user from and `null` for a page of the flow. With
  /// no pages it answers [ShowOver] again, so a router never asks with none
  /// about a location that it shows over a page.
  ///
  /// What a gate made the class remember is forgotten when no gate keeps
  /// the user from the location and it is outside every flow, or in one
  /// that is over: the user has moved on, as when the code of the app
  /// navigates right when a gate starts allowing. Before that, it is
  /// forgotten if a guard that does not bring the user back stopped
  /// allowing, as [changed] says.
  WhenAsked<L>? asked(
    String? route,
    L location, {
    required Iterable<String?> onTopOf,
  }) {
    _forgetAfterStop();
    final guard = _guardKeepingFrom(route);
    if (guard != null && guard.routes == null) {
      if (!_inAFlow(route)) _remembered = (route: route, location: location);
      return ShowInstead(locationOf(guard.redirectTo));
    }
    final over = flowIsOver(route);
    if (over || !_inAFlow(route)) _remembered = null;
    if (over) return ShowInstead(start);
    if (guard == null) {
      final flow = onTopOf.isEmpty ? _openFlowOf(route) : null;
      if (flow == null) return null;
      _below = 0;
      return ShowOver(location, flow: flow);
    }
    if (onTopOf.any(guard.flow.contains)) return const ShowNothing();
    _below = onTopOf.length;
    return ShowOver(locationOf(guard.redirectTo), flow: guard.flow);
  }

  /// What the router does now that a guard started or stopped allowing, or
  /// `null` to leave its pages as they are. With `null` the router changes
  /// nothing: its pages stay, and so do the routes without a page that code
  /// pushed over them, such as a dialog or the screen that the action of an
  /// onboarding step shows.
  ///
  /// [pages] are the pages that the user can get back to, the one on top
  /// first: those of the root navigator and of the selected branch of the
  /// main navigation, each with the full name of its route, its location,
  /// and whether the router can close it on its own and leave the pages
  /// below it as they are. That holds for a page that `push()` showed, and
  /// for one that `replace()` showed in place of such a page. There are
  /// none for a router that has no page yet.
  ///
  /// First, the remembered location is forgotten if a guard that does not
  /// bring the user back (see [RouteGuard.resumes]) stopped allowing: it
  /// allowed when the class was last asked or told, and does not now. That
  /// holds whichever guard decides and whatever the pages are, and for
  /// whichever gate the location was remembered, so that the next user
  /// does not come to a location of the last one. A guard that stops and
  /// allows again without the class being asked or told in between is not
  /// seen to stop.
  ///
  /// The answer is then the first of these:
  /// - [ShowInstead] of the target of the gate that keeps the user from one
  ///   of the pages. If that gate brings the user back, the location below
  ///   the pages that the router can close is then remembered, or [start]
  ///   if it can close them all, unless one is remembered already or it is
  ///   in the flow of a guard.
  /// - with a location remembered: `null` while a gate keeps the user from
  ///   it. Otherwise the location is forgotten, and the answer is
  ///   [ShowInstead] of it, or of [start] if a guard with
  ///   [RouteGuard.routes] keeps the user from it: the class cannot keep a
  ///   request waiting, so the user moves on to [start].
  /// - for the lowest page that has to leave, which is in a flow that is
  ///   over (see [flowIsOver]) or which a guard with [RouteGuard.routes] keeps
  ///   the user from: [ClosePages] of that page and the pages over it, if the
  ///   router can close each of them and a page stays below; and else
  ///   [ShowInstead] of [start]. The request that waits is dropped when the
  ///   pages that close reach below the flow that [asked] opened last: the
  ///   page that the request was made on closes then, as when it asks for
  ///   a condition that stopped holding while the flow of another one was
  ///   open over it.
  ///
  /// So the pages of a flow close once its guards allow, also below a page
  /// that was pushed from them, and a page that asks for a condition
  /// closes when the condition stops holding. A page that took the place
  /// of the stack, as after `go()` to it, has nothing below it: the user
  /// then comes to [start].
  ///
  /// A known limit of the second answer: a location that a guard with
  /// [RouteGuard.routes] keeps the user from, and that was asked for
  /// behind a gate, is lost once the gate allows. A link to such a route
  /// that opens the app on a first launch, behind an onboarding, leaves
  /// the user on [start], though [asked] opens the flow of the guard over
  /// [start] for the same link when no gate is in its way.
  WhenChanged<L>? changed(
    Iterable<({String? route, L location, bool pushed})> pages,
  ) {
    _forgetAfterStop();
    final all = pages.toList();
    for (final page in all) {
      final gate = _guardKeepingFrom(page.route);
      if (gate == null || gate.routes != null) continue;
      final below = all.where((other) => !other.pushed).firstOrNull;
      if (gate.resumes && _remembered == null && !_inAFlow(below?.route)) {
        _remembered = (
          route: below?.route,
          location: below == null ? start : below.location,
        );
      }
      return ShowInstead(locationOf(gate.redirectTo));
    }
    if (_remembered case final remembered?) {
      final guard = _guardKeepingFrom(remembered.route);
      // The pages are those of the flow of the gate, which is not over.
      if (guard != null && guard.routes == null) return null;
      _remembered = null;
      return ShowInstead(guard == null ? remembered.location : start);
    }
    // No gate keeps the user from a page, so a guard that does is one with
    // routes.
    final lowest = all.lastIndexWhere(
      (page) => flowIsOver(page.route) || _guardKeepingFrom(page.route) != null,
    );
    if (lowest < 0) return null;
    final closed = lowest + 1;
    return all.take(closed).every((page) => page.pushed) && closed < all.length
        ? ClosePages(closed, dropsRequest: all.length - closed < _below)
        : ShowInstead(start);
  }

  /// Forgets the remembered location if a guard that does not bring the
  /// user back stopped allowing since the class last looked at the guards,
  /// and notes which of those guards allow now.
  void _forgetAfterStop() {
    for (final guard in routeGuards) {
      if (guard.resumes) continue;
      if (guard.allows.value) {
        _allowed.add(guard);
      } else if (_allowed.remove(guard)) {
        _remembered = null;
      }
    }
  }

  /// Whether [route] is in the flow of a guard, of whichever guard.
  static bool _inAFlow(String? route) =>
      routeGuards.any((guard) => guard.flow.contains(route));

  /// The flow that [route] is a page of and that only guards with
  /// [RouteGuard.routes] keep open: that of the first guard that does not
  /// allow and has the route in its flow; or `null` if there is none, or if
  /// a gate that does not allow has the route in its flow too, since the
  /// flow of a gate takes the place of the stack.
  static Set<String>? _openFlowOf(String? route) {
    Set<String>? flow;
    for (final guard in routeGuards) {
      if (guard.allows.value || !guard.flow.contains(route)) continue;
      if (guard.routes == null) return null;
      flow ??= guard.flow;
    }
    return flow;
  }
}

import 'dart:async';

import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';
import 'package:go_router/go_router.dart';
import 'package:my_app/core/analytics/firebase_analytics_service.dart'
    show logFirebaseScreenView;
import 'package:my_app/core/layout/app_shell.dart' show AppShell;
import 'package:my_app/core/layout/destination.dart' show appDestinations;
import 'package:my_app/features/home/home_screen.dart' as screen0;
import 'package:my_app/features/onboarding/onboarding_screen.dart' as screen2;
import 'package:my_app/features/paywall/paywall_screen.dart' as screen7;
import 'package:my_app/features/paywall/subscription_screen.dart' as screen8;
import 'package:my_app/features/settings/settings_screen.dart' as screen1;
import 'package:my_app/features/sign_in/account_screen.dart' as screen6;
